\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{3}\int_{\Omega}(e^{\prime}(h))^{3}\left((\mathbf{F}^{0}_{k}\cdot\partial)^{2}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{l}\cdot\partial)(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)^{2}h\right)\cdot\left((\mathbf{F}^{0}_{k}\cdot\partial)^{2}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{l}\cdot\partial)\partial_{t}h\right)\,dy

(3.117)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\sum_{j=1}^{3}\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\Omega}\left|(e^{\prime}(h))^{\frac{3}{2}}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{k}\cdot\partial)^{2}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{l}\cdot\partial)(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)h\right|^{2}\,dy

\displaystyle+\sum_{j=1}^{3}\frac{3}{2}\int_{\Omega}(e^{\prime}(h))^{2}e^{\prime\prime}(h)\partial_{t}h\left|(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{k}\cdot\partial)^{2}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{l}\cdot\partial)(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)h\right|^{2}\,dy

\displaystyle-\sum_{j=1}^{3}3\int_{\Omega}(e^{\prime}(h))^{2}e^{\prime\prime}(h)((\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)h)\left((\mathbf{F}^{0}_{k}\cdot\partial)^{2}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{l}\cdot\partial)(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)h\right)\left((\mathbf{F}^{0}_{k}\cdot\partial)^{2}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{l}\cdot\partial)\partial_{t}h\right)\,dy

\displaystyle-\sum_{j=1}^{3}\int_{\Omega}(e^{\prime}(h))^{3}\partial_{m}\mathbf{F}^{0}_{mj}\left((\mathbf{F}^{0}_{k}\cdot\partial)^{2}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{l}\cdot\partial)(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)h\right)\left((\mathbf{F}^{0}_{k}\cdot\partial)^{2}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{l}\cdot\partial)\partial_{t}h\right)\,dy.

Now we analyze the remaining terms. Keep in mind the physical constraints (
1.9
):
|e^{(k)}(h)|\lesssim|e^{\prime}(h)|^{k}
.

In (
3.116
), we have

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}2e^{\prime}(h)e^{\prime\prime}(h)\left(\nabla_{\tilde{a}}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{k}\cdot\partial)^{2}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{l}\cdot\partial)h\right)\partial_{t}\left((\mathbf{F}^{0}_{k}\cdot\partial)^{2}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{l}\cdot\partial)h\right)\,dy

(3.118)

\displaystyle+\int_{\Omega}(e^{\prime}(h))^{2}\left(\nabla_{\tilde{a}}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{k}\cdot\partial)^{2}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{l}\cdot\partial)h\right)\cdot\left(\left[\text{div}_{\tilde{a}},\partial_{t}\right](\mathbf{F}^{0}_{k}\cdot\partial)^{2}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{l}\cdot\partial)h\right)\,dy

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle 2\|\sqrt{e^{\prime}(h)}\|_{L^{\infty}}\left\|e^{\prime}(h)(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{k}\cdot\partial)^{2}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{l}\cdot\partial)h\right\|_{1}\left\|(e^{\prime}(h))^{\frac{3}{2}}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{k}\cdot\partial)^{2}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{l}\cdot\partial)\partial_{t}h\right\|_{0}

\displaystyle+\|\nabla_{\tilde{a}}v\|_{L^{\infty}}\left\|e^{\prime}(h)(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{k}\cdot\partial)^{2}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{l}\cdot\partial)h\right\|_{1}^{2}.

In (
3.117
), we have

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{3}\frac{3}{2}\int_{\Omega}(e^{\prime}(h))^{2}e^{\prime\prime}(h)\partial_{t}h\left|(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{k}\cdot\partial)^{2}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{l}\cdot\partial)(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)h\right|^{2}\,dy

(3.119)

\displaystyle-\sum_{j=1}^{3}3\int_{\Omega}(e^{\prime}(h))^{2}e^{\prime\prime}(h)((\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)h)\left((\mathbf{F}^{0}_{k}\cdot\partial)^{2}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{l}\cdot\partial)(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)h\right)\left((\mathbf{F}^{0}_{k}\cdot\partial)^{2}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{l}\cdot\partial)\partial_{t}h\right)\,dy

\displaystyle-\sum_{j=1}^{3}\int_{\Omega}(e^{\prime}(h))^{3}\partial_{m}\mathbf{F}^{0}_{mj}\left((\mathbf{F}^{0}_{k}\cdot\partial)^{2}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{l}\cdot\partial)(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)h\right)\left((\mathbf{F}^{0}_{k}\cdot\partial)^{2}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{l}\cdot\partial)\partial_{t}h\right)\,dy

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{j=1}^{3}

\displaystyle\|e^{\prime}(h)\partial_{t}h\|_{L^{\infty}}\left\|(e^{\prime}(h))^{\frac{3}{2}}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{k}\cdot\partial)^{2}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{l}\cdot\partial)(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)h\right\|_{0}^{2}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle 3\|e^{\prime}(h)(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)h\|_{L^{\infty}}\left\|(e^{\prime}(h))^{\frac{3}{2}}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{k}\cdot\partial)^{2}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{l}\cdot\partial)(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)h\right\|_{0}\left\|(e^{\prime}(h))^{\frac{3}{2}}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{k}\cdot\partial)^{2}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{l}\cdot\partial)\partial_{t}h\right\|_{0}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle P\left(\|e^{\prime}(h)\|_{L^{\infty}},\left\|(e^{\prime}(h))^{\frac{3}{2}}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{k}\cdot\partial)^{2}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{l}\cdot\partial)(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)h\right\|_{0},\|\mathbf{F}^{0}\|_{4},\|\partial_{t}h\|_{3}\right).

It remains to analyze the last two lines in (
3.116
). The most difficult term is the last line. We should first integrate
\partial_{t}
by parts and use the time integral outside
L_{3}^{j}
to eliminate that time derivative, and then integrate
(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)
by parts to get

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}(e^{\prime}(h))^{2}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{k}\cdot\partial)^{2}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{l}\cdot\partial)\partial_{t}h\cdot\left((\mathbf{F}^{0}_{k}\cdot\partial)^{2}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{l}\cdot\partial)(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\int_{0}^{t}L_{3}^{j}(\tau)\right)\,dyd\tau

(3.120)

\displaystyle\overset{\partial_{t}}{=}

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}(e^{\prime}(h))^{2}\left((\mathbf{F}^{0}_{k}\cdot\partial)^{2}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{l}\cdot\partial)h\right)\cdot\left((\mathbf{F}^{0}_{k}\cdot\partial)^{2}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{l}\cdot\partial)(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)L_{3}^{j}(t)\right)\,dy\,dt

\displaystyle+2\int_{0}^{T}e^{\prime}(h)e^{\prime\prime}(h)\partial_{t}h(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{k}\cdot\partial)^{2}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{l}\cdot\partial)h\cdot\left((\mathbf{F}^{0}_{k}\cdot\partial)^{2}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{l}\cdot\partial)(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\int_{0}^{t}L_{3}^{j}\right)\,dy\,dt

\displaystyle-\int_{\Omega}(e^{\prime}(h))^{2}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{k}\cdot\partial)^{2}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{l}\cdot\partial)h\cdot\left((\mathbf{F}^{0}_{k}\cdot\partial)^{2}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{l}\cdot\partial)(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\int_{0}^{T}L_{3}^{j}\right)\,dy

\displaystyle\overset{(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)}{=}

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}(e^{\prime}(h))^{2}\left((\mathbf{F}^{0}_{k}\cdot\partial)^{2}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{l}\cdot\partial)(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)h\right)\cdot\left((\mathbf{F}^{0}_{k}\cdot\partial)^{2}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{l}\cdot\partial)L_{3}^{j}(t)\right)\,dy\,dt

\displaystyle-2\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}e^{\prime}(h)e^{\prime\prime}(h)\partial_{t}h\left((\mathbf{F}^{0}_{k}\cdot\partial)^{2}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{l}\cdot\partial)(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)h\right)\cdot\left((\mathbf{F}^{0}_{k}\cdot\partial)^{2}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{l}\cdot\partial)\int_{0}^{t}L_{3}^{j}\right)\,dy\,dt

\displaystyle+\int_{\Omega}(e^{\prime}(h))^{2}\left((\mathbf{F}^{0}_{k}\cdot\partial)^{2}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{l}\cdot\partial)(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)h\right)\cdot\left((\mathbf{F}^{0}_{k}\cdot\partial)^{2}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{l}\cdot\partial)\int_{0}^{T}L_{3}^{j}\right)\,dy+\cdots

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle P(\mathbb{E}_{\kappa}(T))\int_{0}^{T}P\left(\left\|(e^{\prime}(h))^{\frac{3}{2}}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{k}\cdot\partial)^{2}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{l}\cdot\partial)(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)h\right\|_{0},\left\|\left((\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\psi,(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\eta,(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\tilde{\eta},\mathbf{F}^{0},v\right)\right\|_{4}\right)\,dt,

where in the omitted terms the derivative falls on either
\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}
(and thus gives a lower order term) or the weight function (and thus gives more weight functions).

Finally we analyze the second last line in (
3.116
). It suffices to control
\sqrt{e^{\prime}(h)}
-weighted
L^{2}
-norm of
L_{42}+(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{k}\cdot\partial)^{2}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{l}\cdot\partial)(L_{1}+L_{2})
.

The
L_{42}
-term is easier to control because at least one derivative falls on the weight function, and all the other terms only contain spatial derivative and thus do not require any weight function.

\displaystyle\left\|\sqrt{e^{\prime}(h)}L_{42}\right\|_{0}

(3.121)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\|\sqrt{e^{\prime}(h)}\left(\left[e^{\prime}(h),(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{k}\cdot\partial)^{2}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{l}\cdot\partial)\right]\partial_{t}^{2}h+\left[(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{k}\cdot\partial)^{2}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{l}\cdot\partial),\Delta_{\tilde{a}}\right]h+\sum_{j=1}^{3}\left[(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{k}\cdot\partial)^{2}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{l}\cdot\partial),e^{\prime}(h)\right](\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)^{2}h\right)\right\|_{0}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle P\left(\left\|\sqrt{e^{\prime}(h)}\right\|_{L^{\infty}},\left\|e^{\prime}(h)\partial_{t}^{2}h\right\|_{0},\|h\|_{4},\|\mathbf{F}^{0}\|_{4}\right).

The term
(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{k}\cdot\partial)^{2}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{l}\cdot\partial)(L_{1}+L_{2})
can be similarly controlled as in (
3.121
)

\displaystyle\left\|\sqrt{e^{\prime}(h)}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{k}\cdot\partial)^{2}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{l}\cdot\partial)(L_{1}+L_{2})\right\|_{0}

(3.122)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left\|\sqrt{e^{\prime}(h)}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{k}\cdot\partial)^{2}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{l}\cdot\partial)\left(-\partial_{t}\tilde{a}^{li}\partial_{l}v_{i}-\nabla_{\tilde{a}}^{i}\left((\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\tilde{\eta}_{l}\right)\nabla_{\tilde{a}}^{l}\left((\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\tilde{\eta}_{i}\right)\right)\right\|_{0}

\displaystyle+\left\|\sqrt{e^{\prime}(h)}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{k}\cdot\partial)^{2}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{l}\cdot\partial)\left(-e^{\prime\prime}(h)(\partial_{t}h)^{2}+\sum_{j=1}^{3}e^{\prime\prime}(h)\left((\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)h\right)^{2}\right)\right\|_{0}

Notice that the leading order terms in
(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{k}\cdot\partial)^{2}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{l}\cdot\partial)(L_{1}+L_{2})
are

(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{k}\cdot\partial)^{2}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{l}\cdot\partial)\left((\nabla_{\tilde{a}}v)(\nabla_{\tilde{a}}v)-(\nabla_{\tilde{a}}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\tilde{\eta})(\nabla_{\tilde{a}}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\tilde{\eta})-e^{\prime\prime}(h)(\partial_{t}h)^{2}+e^{\prime\prime}(h)((\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)h)^{2}\right),

which are all of at most 4 derivatives with at most one time derivative. Therefore, no weight function is needed except the divergence of deformation tensor
\sum\limits_{j=1}^{3}\|e^{\prime}(h)(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)h\|_{3}
. Luckily,
|e^{\prime\prime}(h)|\lesssim|e^{\prime}(h)|^{2}
has provided enough number of weight functions. Hence, we get

\left\|\sqrt{e^{\prime}(h)}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{k}\cdot\partial)^{2}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{l}\cdot\partial)(L_{1}+L_{2})\right\|_{0}\lesssim P\left(\|\mathbf{F}^{0}\|_{4},\|\eta\|_{4},\|v\|_{4},\|(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\tilde{\eta}\|_{4},\|\partial_{t}h\|_{3},\|e^{\prime}(h)(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)h\|_{3}\right).

(3.123)

Summarising (
3.115
)-(
3.123
), we finally get the weighted energy estimates of
(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{k}\cdot\partial)^{2}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{l}\cdot\partial)
-differentiated wave equation (
3.114
) by taking summation on
k,l
,

\displaystyle\sum_{k,l=1}^{3}\left(\left\|(e^{\prime}(h))^{\frac{3}{2}}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{k}\cdot\partial)^{2}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{l}\cdot\partial)\partial_{t}h\right\|_{0}^{2}+\left\|e^{\prime}(h)(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{k}\cdot\partial)^{2}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{l}\cdot\partial)h\right\|_{1}^{2}+\sum_{j=1}^{3}\left\|(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{k}\cdot\partial)^{2}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{l}\cdot\partial)(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)h\right\|_{0}^{2}\right)

(3.124)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{0}+P(\mathbb{E}_{\kappa}(T))\int_{0}^{T}P(\mathbb{E}_{\kappa}(t))\,dt.

3.4.3
Mixed-tangentially-differentiated wave equations

We still need to control the weighted energy estimates of
\sum\limits_{j=1}^{3}\partial_{t}^{2}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)
-differentiated and
\sum\limits_{j=1}^{3}\partial_{t}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)^{2}
-differentiated wave equations. The proof follows in the same way as the previously discussed cases because

1.

The energy estimates must be uniform in sound speed
: Compared with “fully time-differentiated” case, there are more spatial derivatives. Therefore, less number of weight functions will be needed in the commutator estimates.

2.

No loss of derivative
: The number of derivatives in the source terms will be no greater than the previously discusses case and thus there is no loss of derivatives in the source terms. Also, since both
(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)
and
\partial_{t}
are tangential derivatives on the boundary, there is no boundary intergal when integrating by parts.

3.

On the source term
L_{3}^{j}
: The treatment of this term is the same as “fully
(\mathbf{F}^{0}\cdot\partial)
-differentiated” case: First integrating
\partial_{t}
by parts to eliminate one time derivative with the help of time integral as in (
3.120
), then integrating
(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)
by parts to finish the control.

We list the results here

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{3}\left(\left\|(e^{\prime}(h))^{\frac{3}{2}}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{k}\cdot\partial)\partial_{t}^{3}h\right\|_{0}^{2}+\left\|e^{\prime}(h)(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{k}\cdot\partial)\partial_{t}^{2}h\right\|_{1}^{2}+\sum_{j=1}^{3}\left\|(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{k}\cdot\partial)(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\partial_{t}^{2}h\right\|_{0}^{2}\right)

(3.125)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{3}\left(\left\|(e^{\prime}(h))^{\frac{3}{2}}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{k}\cdot\partial)^{2}\partial_{t}^{2}h\right\|_{0}^{2}+\left\|e^{\prime}(h)(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{k}\cdot\partial)^{2}\partial_{t}h\right\|_{1}^{2}+\sum_{j=1}^{3}\left\|(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{k}\cdot\partial)^{2}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\partial_{t}h\right\|_{0}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{0}+P(\mathbb{E}_{\kappa}(T))\int_{0}^{T}P(\mathbb{E}_{\kappa}(t))\,dt.

3.5
A priori estimates of the nonlinear
\kappa
-approximation system

Now we are able to finalize the uniform-in-
\kappa
a priori estimate of the nonlinear approximation system (
3.2
). We recall that (
3.24
) gives the
L^{2}
-estimates, (
3.22
) and (
3.23
) give the div-curl-tangential decomposition for the full spatial derivatives. Then (
3.25
) and (
3.26
) reduce the boundary part to interior tangential estimates which are established by (
3.87
). The inequality (
3.37
) gives the common control of curl part and (
3.39
), (
3.45
) reduce the divergence control to the elliptic estimates of
h
. With the help of Christodoulou-Lindblad
[
14
]
elliptic estimates Lemma
2.6
, we reduce the estimates of
h
to the fully-tangential-differentiated derivatives in (
3.99
). On the other hand, the estimates of time derivatives of
v
and
(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\eta
are again reduced to full spatial derivatives and the estimates of
h
in (
3.101
), (
3.102
) and (
3.103
).

After the reductions above, it remains to control the weighted energy estimates of
h
listed in (
3.99
). This is finished by analyzing the tangentially-differentiated wave equation (
3.89
) of
h
. Finally, we get the desired energy estimates (actually slightly better than our expectation because less weight functions are needed) as in (
3.113
), (
3.124
) and (
3.125
). We taking summation and thus get a nonlinear Gronwall-type inequality

\mathbb{E}_{\kappa}(T)\lesssim\delta^{2}\left(\|v\|_{4}^{2}+\sum_{j=1}^{3}\|(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\eta\|_{4}^{2}\right)+\mathcal{P}_{0}+P(\mathbb{E}_{\kappa}(T))\int_{0}^{T}P(\mathbb{E}_{\kappa}(t))\,dt

(3.126)

under the a priori assumptions (
3.6
)-(
3.7
).

By the argument in Tao
[
82
, Chapter 1.3]
, we can pick a suitably small
T>0
such that the
\delta
-terms can be absorbed to LHS and get the following energy bound

\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\mathbb{E}_{\kappa}(t)\leq P\left(\|v_{0}\|_{4},\|\mathbf{F}^{0}\|_{4},\|\mathfrak{h}_{0}\|_{4}\right).

(3.127)

3.6
Justification of the a priori assumptions

Finally, we need to justify the a priori assumptions (
3.6
) and (
3.7
). The Rayleigh-Taylor sign condition can be directly justified by using Morrey’s embedding. Notice that
\frac{\partial h}{\partial N}\in L^{\infty}([0,T];H^{\frac{5}{2}}(\Gamma)),~\partial_{t}\left(\frac{\partial h}{\partial N}\right)\in L^{\infty}([0,T];H^{\frac{3}{2}}(\Gamma))
and the boundary is
\mathbb{T}^{2}
a bounded open set in 2D. By using Morrey’s embedding, the taylor sign is actually a Hölder continuous function in both
t
and
y
variables:

\frac{\partial h}{\partial N}\in W^{1,\infty}([0,T];H^{\frac{3}{2}}(\Gamma))\hookrightarrow W^{1,\infty}([0,T];W^{1,4}(\Gamma))\hookrightarrow W^{1,4}([0,T]\times\Gamma)\hookrightarrow C_{t,x}^{0,\frac{1}{4}}([0,T]\times\Gamma).

Therefore, the a priori assumption holds is a positive time interval provided that
-\frac{\partial\mathfrak{h}_{0}}{\partial N}\geq c_{0}>0
initially. The second assumption (
3.7
) is easily justified by

\text{Id}-\tilde{a}=\int_{0}^{T}\partial_{t}\tilde{a}\,dt=-\int_{0}^{T}\tilde{a}:\partial\tilde{\eta}:\tilde{a}\,dt,

of which the
H^{3}
-norm is directly bounded by our energy functional. Similar estimate holds for
\tilde{J}-1
since
\tilde{J}
is a bilinear function of
\partial\tilde{\eta}
and
\partial\tilde{\eta}-
Id is sufficiently small due to that of
\tilde{a}-
Id.

4
Construction of solutions to the approximation system

In Section
3
, we have derived the uniform-in-
\kappa
a priori estimates for the nonlinear
\kappa
-approximation system (
3.2
). Now we are going to construct the strong solution to (
3.2
) for each fixed
\kappa>0
. Given the a priori estimates, it is natural to consider linearization and Picard iteration to construct the solution to (
3.2
). Specifically, we expect to start from the trivial solution
(\eta^{(0)},v^{(0)},h^{(0)})=(\eta^{(1)},v^{(1)},h^{(1)})=(\text{Id},0,0)
and inductively define
(\eta^{(n+1)},v^{(n+1)},h^{(n+1)})
provided that
\{(\eta^{(k)},v^{(k)},h^{(k)})\}_{0\leq k\leq n}
are given

\begin{cases}\partial_{t}\eta^{(n+1)}=v^{(n+1)}+\psi^{(n)}~~~&\text{in }\Omega,\\
\partial_{t}v^{(n+1)}=-\nabla_{\tilde{a}^{(n)}}h^{(n+1)}+\sum\limits_{j=1}^{3}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)^{2}\eta^{(n+1)}~~~&\text{in }\Omega,\\
\text{div }_{\tilde{a}^{(n)}}v^{(n+1)}=-e^{\prime}(h^{(n)})\partial_{t}h^{(n+1)}~~~&\text{in }\Omega,\\
\text{div }\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}:=\partial_{k}\mathbf{F}^{0}_{kj}=-e^{\prime}(\mathfrak{h}_{0})(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\mathfrak{h}_{0}~~~&\text{in }\Omega,\\
h^{(n+1)}=0,~\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot N=0~~~&\text{on }\Gamma,\\
(\eta^{(n+1)},v^{(n+1)},h^{(n+1)})|_{t=0}=(\text{Id},v_{0},\mathfrak{h}_{0}),\end{cases}

(4.1)

where
\tilde{\eta}^{(n)}
is the smoothed version of
\eta^{(n)}
and
\tilde{a}^{(n)}:=[\partial\tilde{\eta}^{(n)}]^{-1}
. For simplicity on notation, we denote
(\mathring{\eta},\mathring{a},\mathring{v},\mathring{h})=(\eta^{(n)},a^{(n)},v^{(n)},h^{(n)})
and
(\eta,v,h):=(\eta^{(n+1)},v^{(n+1)},h^{(n+1)})
. Then the linearized system becomes

\begin{cases}\partial_{t}\eta=v+\mathring{\psi}~~~&\text{in }\Omega,\\
\partial_{t}v=-\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}h+\sum\limits_{j=1}^{3}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)^{2}\eta~~~&\text{in }\Omega,\\
\text{div}_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}v=-e^{\prime}(\mathring{h})\partial_{t}h~~~&\text{in }\Omega,\\
\text{div }\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}:=\partial_{k}\mathbf{F}^{0}_{kj}=-e^{\prime}(\mathfrak{h}_{0})(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\mathfrak{h}_{0}~~~&\text{in }\Omega,\\
h=0,~\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot N=0~~~&\text{on }\Gamma,\\
(\eta,v,h)|_{t=0}=(\text{Id},v_{0},\mathfrak{h}_{0}).\end{cases}

(4.2)

What we need to do are

1.

Construct the unique strong solution to the linearized
\kappa
-approximation system (
4.2
) for each fixed
\kappa>0
.

2.

Derive the uniform-in-
n
estimates for the linearized
\kappa
-apporximation system (
4.2
) for each fixed
\kappa>0
.

3.

Picard iteration: Prove the sequence
\{(\eta^{(n)},v^{(n)},h^{(n)})\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
strongly converges (subsequentially) in suitable Sobolev spaces to derive the unique strong solution to the nonlinear
\kappa
-approximation system for each fixed
\kappa>0
.

We will prove the following conclusions by verifying the steps above

Proposition 4.1

(
Local well-posedness of the linearized approximation system (
4.2
)
)
.

Fix
\kappa>0
. There exists a time
T_{\kappa}:=T_{\kappa}(\|\partial\mathring{\tilde{{A}}}\|_{L^{\infty}},\|\partial_{t}h\|_{L^{\infty}},\|\mathbf{F}^{0}\|_{3},\|(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\mathring{\psi}\|_{1})>0
such that the linearized
\kappa
-approximation system (
4.2
) has a unique strong solution
(\eta,v,h)
in
[0,T_{\kappa}]
satisfying the
H^{1}
-estimate

\sup_{0\leq t\leq T_{\kappa}}\|v\|_{1}^{2}+\|h\|_{1}^{2}+\sum_{j=1}^{3}\|(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\eta\|_{1}^{2}\leq 2(\|v_{0}\|_{1}^{2}+\|\mathbf{F}^{0}\|_{1}^{2}+\|\mathfrak{h}_{0}\|_{1}^{2}).

(4.3)

\square

Proposition 4.2

(
Uniform-in-
n
estimates of the linearized approximation system (
4.2
)
)
.

Fix
\kappa>0
. There exists a time
T_{\kappa}>0
, such that the unique strong solution
(\eta,v,h)
to the linearized
\kappa
-approximation system (
4.2
) satisfies the following estimates

\sup_{0\leq t\leq T_{\kappa}}\mathring{\mathbb{E}}_{\kappa}(t)\leq P\left(\|v_{0}\|_{4},\|\mathbf{F}^{0}\|_{4},\|\mathfrak{h}_{0}\|_{4}\right),

(4.4)

where

\displaystyle\mathring{\mathbb{E}}_{\kappa}(T)

\displaystyle:=\left\|\eta\right\|_{4}^{2}+\left\|v\right\|_{4}^{2}+\sum\limits_{j=1}^{3}\left\|(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\eta\right\|_{4}^{2}+\left\|h\right\|_{4}^{2}

(4.5)

\displaystyle+\left\|\partial_{t}v\right\|_{3}^{2}+\sum\limits_{j=1}^{3}\left\|(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\partial_{t}\eta\right\|_{3}^{2}+\left\|\partial_{t}h\right\|_{3}^{2}

\displaystyle+\left\|\partial_{t}^{2}v\right\|_{2}^{2}+\sum\limits_{j=1}^{3}\left\|(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\partial_{t}^{2}\eta\right\|_{2}^{2}+\left\|(e^{\prime}(\mathring{h}))^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{t}^{2}h\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle+\left\|(e^{\prime}(\mathring{h}))^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{t}^{3}v\right\|_{1}^{2}+\sum\limits_{j=1}^{3}\left\|(e^{\prime}(\mathring{h}))^{\frac{1}{2}}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\partial_{t}^{3}\eta\right\|_{1}^{2}+\left\|e^{\prime}(\mathring{h})\partial_{t}^{3}h\right\|_{1}^{2}

\displaystyle+\left\|e^{\prime}(\mathring{h})\partial_{t}^{4}v\right\|_{0}^{2}+\sum\limits_{j=1}^{3}\left\|e^{\prime}(\mathring{h})(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\partial_{t}^{4}\eta\right\|_{0}^{2}+\left\|(e^{\prime}(\mathring{h}))^{\frac{3}{2}}\partial_{t}^{4}h\right\|_{0}^{2}.

\square

Proposition 4.3

(
Local well-posedness of the nonlinear
\kappa
-approximation system (
3.2
)
)
.

Fix
\kappa>0
. There exists a time
T_{\kappa}>0
such that the nonlinear
\kappa
-approximation system (
3.2
) has a unique strong solution
(\eta(\kappa),v(\kappa),h(\kappa))
in
[0,T_{\kappa}]
satisfying the following estimates

\sup_{0\leq t\leq T_{\kappa}}\mathbb{E}_{\kappa}(t)\leq P\left(\|v_{0}\|_{4},\|\mathbf{F}^{0}\|_{4},\|\mathfrak{h}_{0}\|_{4}\right).

(4.6)

\square

4.1
Failure of fixed-point argument to solve linearized system

Before going to the proof, we would like to point out the specific reasons for the failure of fixed-point argument. In elastodynamics (resp. MHD), the presence of Cauchy-Green tensor
\sum\limits_{j=1}^{3}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)^{2}\eta
(resp. Lorentz force) makes the second equation of (
4.2
) lose one derivative, which tells the essential difference from Euler equations. We are able to avoid such problem in the nonlinear a priori estimates because one can integrate
(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)
by parts in curl and tangential estimates, and re-produce the divergence by
\text{div}_{\tilde{a}}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\eta\approx-e^{\prime}(h)(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)h+\int_{0}^{T}
(controllable terms)
\,dt
. But in the fixed-point argument (for both Banach fixed-point theorem and Tikhonov fixed-point theorem) to solve the linearized equation, we have to estimate
\|v\|_{4}
by
\|v\|_{4}=\|v_{0}\|_{4}+\int_{0}^{T}\|\partial_{t}v(t)\|_{4}\,dt
and thus the
H^{4}
-norms of
\sum\limits_{j=1}^{3}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)^{2}\eta
and
\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}h
are necessary to be controlled. In Gu-Wang
[
31
]
and Zhang
[
94
]
for incompressible MHD and elastodynamics, they introduced a directional viscosity term in the equation of flow map as follows which does compensate the derivative loss caused by the Cauchy-Green tensor.

\partial_{t}\eta-\mu\sum\limits_{j=1}^{3}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)^{2}\eta=v+\mathring{\psi}

(4.7)

However, one also has to derive the estimate for passing the vanishing viscosity limit
\mu\to 0_{+}
to (
4.2
). When estimating the
H^{3}
-norm of
\text{div}_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}((\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\eta)
, one needs to take
\partial^{3}\text{div}_{\tilde{a}}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)
in (
4.7
) and thus the RHS yields
\partial^{3}\text{div}_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}((\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)v)
which loses one derivative. If the fluid is incompressible, then one can directly commute
\text{div}_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}
with
(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)
to eliminate such higher order term. (See Gu-Wang
[
31
, (5.38)]
and Zhang
[
94
, (3.29)]
.) In the compressible case, we have to find other ways to control the divergence. Recall that our previous a priori estimate for the nonlinear system (
3.2
) strongly depends on the fact that the difference between
\text{div}_{\tilde{a}}((\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\eta)
and
-e^{\prime}(h)(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)h
is controllable. But now the divergence of deformation tensor becomes

\text{div}_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\eta=-e^{\prime}(\mathring{h})(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)h+\int_{0}^{T}\text{controllable terms}+\mu\int_{0}^{T}\sum_{k=1}^{3}\text{div}_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{k}\cdot\partial)^{2}\eta,

where we have to control the
H^{3}
-norm of
\mu\text{div}_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{k}\cdot\partial)^{2}\eta
. Unfortunately, the directional viscosity only gives us the
L_{t}^{2}H_{x}^{4}
norm of
\mu(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{k}\cdot\partial)^{2}\eta
and thus the expected “error term” cannot be controlled. Therefore, the “vanishing directional viscosity method” in
[
31
,
94
]
is obviously not applicable to compressible elastodynamics, which leads to the failure of fixed-point argument.

4.2
Hyperbolic approach to solve the linearized approximation system

First we need the following bounds for the coefficients
\mathring{a},\mathring{\eta},\mathring{J}
provided that they hold for
\{(\eta^{(k)},v^{(k)},h^{(k)})\}_{0\leq k\leq n-1}
.

Lemma 4.4
.

For fixed
\kappa>0
, there exists
0<\delta\ll 1
and
A>0
such that
\forall T\in(0,T_{\kappa})

\displaystyle\mathring{\psi},\partial_{t}\mathring{\psi},(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\mathring{\psi},\partial_{t}\mathring{\eta},(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\mathring{\eta}\in

\displaystyle L^{\infty}([0,T];H^{4}(\Omega)),

(4.8)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}^{2}\mathring{\psi},\partial_{t}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\mathring{\psi},\partial_{t}^{2}\mathring{\eta},\partial_{t}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\mathring{\eta},\partial_{t}\mathring{h}\in

\displaystyle L^{\infty}([0,T];H^{3}(\Omega)),

(4.9)

\displaystyle\sqrt{e^{\prime}(\mathring{h})}\partial_{t}^{3}\mathring{\psi},\partial_{t}^{2}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\psi,\partial_{t}^{3}\mathring{\eta},\partial_{t}^{2}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\mathring{\eta},\sqrt{e^{\prime}(\mathring{h})}\partial_{t}^{2}\mathring{h}\in

\displaystyle L^{\infty}([0,T];H^{2}(\Omega)),

(4.10)

\displaystyle e^{\prime}(\mathring{h})\partial_{t}^{4}\mathring{\psi},\sqrt{e^{\prime}(\mathring{h})}\partial_{t}^{3}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\mathring{\psi},\sqrt{e^{\prime}(\mathring{h})}\partial_{t}^{4}\mathring{\eta},\sqrt{e^{\prime}(\mathring{h})}\partial_{t}^{3}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\mathring{\eta},e^{\prime}(\mathring{h})\partial_{t}^{3}\mathring{h}\in

\displaystyle L^{\infty}([0,T];H^{1}(\Omega)),

(4.11)

\displaystyle e^{\prime}(\mathring{h})\partial_{t}^{4}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\mathring{\psi},e^{\prime}(\mathring{h})\partial_{t}^{5}\mathring{\eta},e^{\prime}(\mathring{h})\partial_{t}^{4}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\mathring{\eta},(e^{\prime}(h))^{\frac{3}{2}}\partial_{t}^{4}\mathring{h}\in

\displaystyle L^{\infty}([0,T];L^{2}(\Omega)).

(4.12)

\displaystyle\|\mathring{J}-1\|_{3}+\|\mathring{\tilde{{J}}}-1\|_{3}+\|\text{Id}-\mathring{a}\|_{3}+\|\text{Id}-\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}\|_{3}\leq

\displaystyle\delta.

(4.13)

Proof.

The proof follows in the same way as Lemma
3.2
and the justification of (
3.7
) in Section
3.6
.
∎

Now let us rewrite the system (
4.2
) to be the following system by denoting
\mathfrak{F}^{i}_{j}=(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\eta^{i}
and
\mathfrak{F}_{j}=((\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\eta^{1},(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\eta^{2},(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\eta^{3})
,
\mathfrak{F}^{i}=\left((\mathbf{F}^{0}_{1}\cdot\partial)\eta_{i},(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{2}\cdot\partial)\eta_{i},(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{3}\cdot\partial)\eta_{i}\right)

\begin{cases}\partial_{t}\mathfrak{F}_{j}=(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)v+\mathring{\psi}~~~&\text{in }\Omega,\\
\partial_{t}v+\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}h-\sum\limits_{j=1}^{3}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\mathfrak{F}_{j}=0~~~&\text{in }\Omega,\\
e^{\prime}(\mathring{h})\partial_{t}h+\text{div}_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}v=0~~~&\text{in }\Omega,\\
\text{div }\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}:=\partial_{k}\mathbf{F}^{0}_{kj}=-e^{\prime}(\mathfrak{h}_{0})(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\mathfrak{h}_{0}~~~&\text{in }\Omega,\\
h=0,~\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot N=0~~~&\text{on }\Gamma,\\
(\eta,v,h)|_{t=0}=(\text{Id},v_{0},\mathfrak{h}_{0}).\end{cases}

(4.14)

Note that once
\mathfrak{F},v,h
are solved, the flow map
\eta
is automatically solved by
\partial_{t}\eta=v+\mathring{\psi}
. Therefore it remains to solve (
4.14
). We write (
4.14
) into a first-order symmetric hyperbolic system of the variable
\mathbf{X}\in\mathbb{R}^{13}
defined by

\mathbf{X}:=\left[h,v,\mathfrak{F}^{1},\mathfrak{F}^{2},\mathfrak{F}^{3}\right]^{\top}

(4.15)

A_{0}(t,y)\partial_{t}\mathbf{X}+\sum_{l=1}^{3}A_{l}(t,y)\partial_{l}\mathbf{X}=f.

(4.16)

Here

f(t,y)=\left[\mathbf{0}_{1\times 4},(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{1}\cdot\partial)\mathring{\psi}_{1},(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{2}\cdot\partial)\mathring{\psi}_{1},(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{3}\cdot\partial)\mathring{\psi}_{1},(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{1}\cdot\partial)\mathring{\psi}_{2},(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{2}\cdot\partial)\mathring{\psi}_{2},(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{3}\cdot\partial)\mathring{\psi}_{2},(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{1}\cdot\partial)\mathring{\psi}_{3},(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{2}\cdot\partial)\mathring{\psi}_{3},(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{3}\cdot\partial)\mathring{\psi}_{3}\right]^{\top},

(4.17)

A_{0}(t,y)=\text{diag}\left[e^{\prime}(\mathring{h}),1,\cdots,1\right],

(4.18)

and
A_{l}(t,y)
equals to the following matrix

\begin{bmatrix}0&\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{l1}&\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{l2}&\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{l3}&0&0&0&0&0&0&0&0&0\\
\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{l1}&&&&-\mathbf{F}^{0}_{l1}&-\mathbf{F}^{0}_{l2}&-\mathbf{F}^{0}_{l3}&0&0&0&0&0&0\\
\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{l2}&&\mathbf{O}_{3\times 3}&&0&0&0&-\mathbf{F}^{0}_{l1}&-\mathbf{F}^{0}_{l2}&-\mathbf{F}^{0}_{l3}&0&0&0\\
\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{l3}&&&&0&0&0&0&0&0&-\mathbf{F}^{0}_{l1}&-\mathbf{F}^{0}_{l2}&-\mathbf{F}^{0}_{l3}\\
0&-\mathbf{F}^{0}_{l1}&0&0&&&&&&&&&&\\
0&-\mathbf{F}^{0}_{l2}&0&0&&&&&&&&&&\\
0&-\mathbf{F}^{0}_{l3}&0&0&&&&&&&&&&\\
0&0&-\mathbf{F}^{0}_{l1}&0&&&&&&&&&&\\
0&0&-\mathbf{F}^{0}_{l2}&0&&&&&&\mathbf{O}_{9\times 9}&&&&\\
0&0&-\mathbf{F}^{0}_{l3}&0&&&&&&&&&&\\
0&0&0&-\mathbf{F}^{0}_{l1}&&&&&&&&&&\\
0&0&0&-\mathbf{F}^{0}_{l2}&&&&&&&&&&\\
0&0&0&-\mathbf{F}^{0}_{l3}&&&&&&&&&&\\
\end{bmatrix}.

(4.19)

Then we find that, the normal projection of the coefficient matrix

A_{N}:=\sum_{l=1}^{3}A_{l}N_{l}=\pm\begin{bmatrix}0&\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{l1}&\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{l2}&\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{l3}&&&\\
\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{l1}&&&&&&\\
\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{l2}&&\mathbf{O}_{3\times 3}&&&&\\
\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{l3}&&&&&&\\
&&&&&&&\\
&&&&&\mathbf{O}_{9\times 9}&\\
&&&&&&&\\
\end{bmatrix}\text{ similar to }\pm\begin{bmatrix}0&0&0&1&&&\\
0&&&&&&\\
0&&\mathbf{O}_{3\times 3}&&&&\\
1&&&&&&\\
&&&&&&&\\
&&&&&\mathbf{O}_{9\times 9}&\\
&&&&&&&\\
\end{bmatrix}

which is of constant rank and singular on the boundary. So the linearized system (
4.14
) is indeed of characteristic boundary conditions. By the argument in Lax-Phillips
[
46
]
, we need to prove the following things in order for the existence of
L^{2}
-solution to (
4.14
),

1.

Derive the
L^{2}
-a priori bound for (
4.16
) without loss of regularity.

2.

Find the dual problem of (
4.16
).

3.

Derive the
L^{2}
-a priori bound for the dual problem without loss of regularity.

The
L^{2}
-a priori bound for (
4.16
) is quite straightforward. We take
L^{2}
inner product of
v
and the second equation of (
4.14
) to get

\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\Omega}|v|^{2}\,dy+\int_{\Omega}\left(\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}h\right)\cdot v\,dy-\sum_{l=1}^{3}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{l}\cdot\partial)\mathfrak{F}^{i}\cdot v_{i}\,dy=0

(4.20)

Integrating by parts in the second and third term, and invoking the boundary conditions
h=0,\mathbf{F}^{0}\cdot N=\mathbf{0}
, we know

\int_{\Omega}\left(\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}h\right)\cdot v\,dy=\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\Omega}e^{\prime}(\mathring{h})|h|^{2}\,dy-\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega}e^{\prime\prime}(\mathring{h})\partial_{t}\mathring{h}|h|^{2}\,dy-\int_{\Omega}\partial_{l}\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{li}v_{i}\cdot h\,dy,

(4.21)

and

\displaystyle-\sum_{l=1}^{3}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{l}\cdot\partial)\mathfrak{F}^{i}\cdot v_{i}\,dy=\sum_{l=1}^{3}\int_{\Omega}\mathfrak{F}^{i}\cdot(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{l}\cdot\partial)v_{i}\,dy+\int_{\Omega}\left(\text{div }\mathbf{F}^{0}_{l}\right)\mathfrak{F}\cdot v\,dy

(4.22)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{l=1}^{3}\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\Omega}\left|(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{l}\cdot\partial)\eta\right|^{2}\,dy-\sum_{l=1}^{3}\int_{\Omega}\mathfrak{F}^{i}\cdot(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{l}\cdot\partial)\mathring{\psi}_{i}\,dy+\int_{\Omega}\left(\text{div }\mathbf{F}^{0}_{l}\right)\mathfrak{F}\cdot v\,dy.

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\left(\left\|v\right\|_{0}^{2}+\left\|\sqrt{e^{\prime}(\mathring{h})}h\right\|_{0}^{2}+\sum_{l=1}^{3}\left\|\mathfrak{F}_{l}\right\|_{0}^{2}\right)\lesssim\|\partial\mathbf{F}^{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\mathfrak{F}\|_{0}\|v\|_{0}+\sum_{l=1}^{3}\|\mathfrak{F}\|_{0}\left\|(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{l}\cdot\partial)\psi\right\|_{0}+\frac{1}{2}\|e^{\prime\prime}(\mathring{h})\partial_{t}\mathring{h}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|h\|_{0}^{2}+\|\partial\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|v\|_{0}\|h\|_{0},

(4.23)

and thus by Gronwall inequality we are able to get the
L^{2}
-a priori bound

\left\|v\right\|_{0}^{2}+\left\|\sqrt{e^{\prime}(\mathring{h})}h\right\|_{0}^{2}+\sum_{l=1}^{3}\left\|\mathfrak{F}_{l}\right\|_{0}^{2}\bigg|^{T}_{0}\lesssim C\left(\|\mathbf{F}^{0}\|_{3},\|e^{\prime\prime}(\mathring{h})\partial_{t}\mathring{h}\|_{L^{\infty}},\|\partial\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}\|_{L^{\infty}}\right)\|f\|_{0}.

(4.24)

Next we derive the dual problem of (
4.16
). We introduce the “test function”
\mathbf{Y}\in\mathbb{R}^{13}
as the variable of the dual problem by

\mathbf{Y}:=\left[\theta,w,\mathring{\mathbf{G}}^{1},\mathring{\mathbf{G}}^{2},\mathring{\mathbf{G}}^{3}\right]^{\top},

(4.25)

where the definition of
\mathring{\mathbf{G}}^{i}
is the similar as
\mathfrak{F}^{i}
.

Testing (
4.16
) with
\mathbf{Y}
under space-time intergal, we can get the following first-order hyperbolic system

A_{0}(t,y)\partial_{t}\mathbf{Y}+\sum_{l=1}^{3}A_{l}(t,y)\partial_{l}\mathbf{Y}=f^{*},

(4.26)

where
f^{*}:=\left[f^{*}_{1},\cdots,f^{*}_{13}\right]^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{13}
is defined by

\displaystyle f^{*}_{1}=

\displaystyle-\partial_{l}\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{li}w_{i}

\displaystyle[f^{*}_{2},f^{*}_{3},f^{*}_{4}]=

\displaystyle\left[\sum_{l=1}^{3}\text{div }\mathbf{F}^{0}_{l}\cdot\mathring{\mathbf{G}}_{1}^{l}-\partial_{l}\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{l1}\theta,\sum_{l=1}^{3}\text{div }\mathbf{F}^{0}_{l}\cdot\mathring{\mathbf{G}}_{2}^{l}-\partial_{l}\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{l2}\theta,\sum_{l=1}^{3}\text{div }\mathbf{F}^{0}_{l}\cdot\mathring{\mathbf{G}}_{3}^{l}-\partial_{l}\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{l3}\theta\right]

\displaystyle[f^{*}_{5},f^{*}_{6},f^{*}_{7}]=

\displaystyle\left[-\text{div }\mathbf{F}^{0}_{1}w^{1}+(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{1}\cdot\partial\psi^{1}),-\text{div }\mathbf{F}^{0}_{2}w^{1}+(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{2}\cdot\partial\psi^{1}),-\text{div }\mathbf{F}^{0}_{3}w^{1}+(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{3}\cdot\partial\psi^{1})\right]

\displaystyle[f^{*}_{8},f^{*}_{9},f^{*}_{10}]=

\displaystyle\left[-\text{div }\mathbf{F}^{0}_{1}w^{2}+(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{1}\cdot\partial\psi^{2}),-\text{div }\mathbf{F}^{0}_{2}w^{2}+(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{2}\cdot\partial\psi^{2}),-\text{div }\mathbf{F}^{0}_{3}w^{2}+(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{3}\cdot\partial\psi^{2})\right]

\displaystyle[f^{*}_{11},f^{*}_{12},f^{*}_{13}]=

\displaystyle\left[-\text{div }\mathbf{F}^{0}_{1}w^{3}+(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{1}\cdot\partial\psi^{3}),-\text{div }\mathbf{F}^{0}_{2}w^{3}+(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{2}\cdot\partial\psi^{3}),-\text{div }\mathbf{F}^{0}_{3}w^{3}+(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{3}\cdot\partial\psi^{3})\right].

Compared with the expression of
f
in (
4.17
), the extra terms are all cause by integrating
(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)
by parts and
\text{div }\mathbf{F}^{0}\neq 0
and are all of the form
\text{div }\mathbf{F}^{0}\cdot w,~\partial\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}\cdot w,~\partial\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}\cdot\theta
or
\text{div }\mathbf{F}^{0}\cdot\mathring{\mathbf{G}}
. Therefore, we can exactly mimic the proof for (
4.16
) to get the
L^{2}
-a priori bound with no loss of regularity of (
4.26
) as

\left\|w\right\|_{0}^{2}+\left\|\sqrt{e^{\prime}(\mathring{h})}\theta\right\|_{0}^{2}+\sum_{l=1}^{3}\left\|\mathring{\mathbf{G}}_{l}\right\|_{0}^{2}\lesssim C\left(\|\mathbf{F}^{0}\|_{3},\|e^{\prime\prime}(\mathring{h})\partial_{t}\mathring{h}\|_{L^{\infty}}\right)\|f^{*}\|_{0}.

(4.27)

Combining (
4.24
) and (
4.27
), we know the system (
4.14
) and thus (
4.2
) has a
L^{2}
-solution. We are able to verify it is the unique strong solution by
H^{1}
-estimates. Here we only show the tangential control, while the normal derivative can be estimated by div-curl decomposition which is exactly the same as what will be done in Section
4.3
.

The tangential part of
H^{1}
-estimate does not need Alinhac good unknown method because the coefficient
\mathring{\tilde{{A}}}
is now
C^{\infty}
and
\kappa>0
is fixed. Taking
\overline{\partial}{}
in the second equation of (
4.2
), we get

\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\Omega}\left|\overline{\partial}{}v\right|^{2}\,dy=-\int_{\Omega}\overline{\partial}{}v_{i}\overline{\partial}{}\left(\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{li}\partial_{l}h\right)\,dy+\sum_{j=1}^{3}\int_{\Omega}\overline{\partial}{}\left((\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)^{2}\eta\right)\cdot\overline{\partial}{}v\,dy

(4.28)

Integrating
(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)
by parts in the second term yields that

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\overline{\partial}{}\left((\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)^{2}\eta\right)\cdot\overline{\partial}{}v\,dy

(4.29)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\int_{\Omega}\overline{\partial}{}\left((\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\eta\right)\cdot\overline{\partial}{}\left((\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\partial_{t}\eta\right)\,dy+\int_{\Omega}\overline{\partial}{}\left((\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\eta\right)\cdot\overline{\partial}{}\left((\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\mathring{\psi}\right)\,dy+\int_{\Omega}(\text{div }\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j})\overline{\partial}{}\left((\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\eta\right)\cdot\overline{\partial}{}v\,dy

\displaystyle+\int_{\Omega}\left[\overline{\partial}{},(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\right](\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\eta\cdot\overline{\partial}{}v\,dy+\int_{\Omega}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\eta\cdot\left[\overline{\partial}{},(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\right]\overline{\partial}{}v\,dy

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\left\|\overline{\partial}{}\left((\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\eta\right)\right\|_{0}^{2}+P\left(\|(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\eta\|_{1},\|(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\mathring{\psi}\|_{1},\|\partial\mathbf{F}^{0}\|_{L^{\infty}},\|v\|_{1}\right)

Integrating by parts in the first term and invoking Piola’s identity gives that

\displaystyle-\int_{\Omega}\overline{\partial}{}v_{i}\overline{\partial}{}\left(\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{li}\partial_{l}h\right)\,dy

(4.30)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}(\text{div}_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}v)\overline{\partial}{}h\,dy-\int_{\Gamma}\overline{\partial}{}v_{i}\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{li}N_{l}\underbrace{\overline{\partial}{}h}_{=0}\,dS

\displaystyle-\int_{\Omega}\overline{\partial}{}v_{i}\cdot\left[\overline{\partial}{},\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{li}\right]\partial_{l}h\,dy-\int_{\Omega}\left[\overline{\partial}{},\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{li}\right]\partial_{l}v_{i}\cdot\overline{\partial}{}h\,dy+\int_{\Omega}\overline{\partial}{}v_{i}\partial_{l}\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{li}\overline{\partial}{}h\,dy

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\left\|\sqrt{e^{\prime}(\mathring{h})}\overline{\partial}{}h\right\|_{0}^{2}+P\left(\|\overline{\partial}{}\mathring{\tilde{{A}}}\|_{L^{\infty}},\|v\|_{1},\|h\|_{1},\|\partial_{t}\mathring{h}\|_{L^{\infty}}\right)

Combining (
4.28
)-(
4.30
), we can get the
H^{1}
-tangential estimates of (
4.2
). The div-curl estimate follows in the same way as in the Section
4.3
so we omit the proof here. Then
\|h\|_{1}
can be estimated by the linearized wave equation derived by taking divergence in the second equation of (
4.2
). Such step is a direct consequence of
L^{2}
-estimate of (
4.48
) so we also skip the proof. Therefore we get the
H^{1}
-estimates without loss of regularity for the linearized
\kappa
-approximation problem (
4.2
), which demonstrates the solution we constructed above is a strong solution and also unique. Proposition
4.1
is proven.

4.3
Uniform estimates of the linearized approximation system

Now we inductively prove the uniform-in-
n
estimates for the linearized
\kappa
-approximation system (
4.2
). WSuppose we have already have the energy estimates for
0\leq k\leq n
and thus Lemma
4.4
holds true for the coefficients of
(n+1)
-th linearized system.

4.3.1
Estimates of velocity

The estimates of velocity is still based on the div-curl-tangential estimates and elliptic estimates of
h
. First we have

\|v\|_{4}\lesssim\|v\|_{0}+\|\text{div }v\|_{3}+\|\text{curl }v\|_{3}+\left|\overline{\partial}{}v\cdot N\right|_{5/2}.

(4.31)

Curl estimates:

The curl estimate follows the same way as in Section
3.1
. Taking
\text{curl}_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}
in the second equation of (
4.14
) we get

\partial_{t}\left(\text{curl}_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}v\right)-\sum\limits_{j=1}^{3}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\left(\text{curl}_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}((\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\eta)\right)=\text{curl }_{\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}_{t}}v+\sum\limits_{j=1}^{3}\left[\text{curl}_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}},(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\right](\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\eta.

(4.32)

Then we take
\partial^{3}
, multiply
\partial^{3}\text{curl}_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}v
, integrate by parts and invoke
\partial_{t}\eta=v+\mathring{\psi}
to get

\displaystyle\left\|\text{curl }v(T)\right\|_{3}^{2}+\sum_{j=1}^{3}\left\|\text{curl }(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\eta(T)\right\|_{3}^{2}\lesssim\delta^{2}\left(\|v(T)\|_{4}^{2}+\sum_{j=1}^{3}\left\|(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\eta\right\|_{4}^{2}\right)+\mathcal{P}_{0}+\int_{0}^{T}P(\mathring{\mathbb{E}}_{\kappa}(t))\,dt.

(4.33)

Tangential estimates:

The boundary term in (
4.31
) is again reduced to
\|\overline{\partial}{}^{4}v\|_{0}
. We still use the Alinhac good unknown method by introducing

\mathring{\mathbf{V}}:=\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}v-\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\mathring{\tilde{{\eta}}}\cdot\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}v,~~\mathring{\mathbf{H}}:=\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}h-\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\mathring{\tilde{{\eta}}}\cdot\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}h.

Applying
\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}
to the second equation in the linearization system (
4.14
), one gets

\partial_{t}\mathring{\mathbf{V}}-\sum_{j=1}^{3}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\left(\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\eta\right)+\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}\mathring{\mathbf{H}}=\partial_{t}(\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\mathring{\tilde{{\eta}}}\cdot\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}v)-\mathring{C}(h)+\sum\limits_{j=1}^{3}\left[\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta},(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\right]((\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\eta),

(4.34)

subject to the boundary condition

\mathring{\mathbf{H}}=-\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\mathring{\tilde{{\eta}}}_{\beta}\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{3\beta}\partial_{3}h~~~\text{ on }\Gamma,

(4.35)

and the corresponding compressibility condition

\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}\cdot\mathring{\mathbf{V}}=\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}(\text{div}_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}v)-\mathring{C}^{\alpha}(v_{\alpha}),~~~\text{in }\Omega.

(4.36)

Here for any function
f
, the comuutator
\mathring{C}(f)
is defined in the same way as before but replacing
\tilde{a}
by
\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}
:

\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}(\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}f)=\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}\mathring{\mathbf{f}}+\mathring{C}(f),

(4.37)

with

\|\mathring{C}(f)\|_{0}\lesssim P(\|\mathring{\eta}\|_{4})\|\partial f\|_{3}.

(4.38)

Here
\mathring{\mathbf{f}}
is the Alinhac good unknown for
f
.

Similarly we have

\|\overline{\partial}{}^{4}f(t)\|_{0}\lesssim\|\mathring{\mathbf{f}}\|_{0}+P(\|f(0)\|_{3})+P(\|\mathring{\eta}\|_{4})\int_{0}^{t}P(\|\partial_{t}f(\tau)\|_{3})~d\tau.

(4.39)

Now we take
L^{2}
inner product between (
4.34
) and
\mathring{\mathbf{V}}
to get the followin analogous estimates

\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\Omega}|\mathring{\mathbf{V}}|^{2}\,dy+\sum_{j=1}^{3}\int_{\Omega}\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\left((\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\eta\right)\cdot(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\mathring{\mathbf{V}}=-\int_{\Omega}\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}\mathring{\mathbf{H}}\cdot\mathring{\mathbf{V}}\,dy+\int_{\Omega}(\text{RHS of }\eqref{goodlinearl})\cdot\mathring{\mathbf{V}}\,dy,

(4.40)

where “RHS” can be directly controlled as in the nonlinear counterpart. As for the first term, we integrate by parts to get

-\int_{\Omega}\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}\mathring{\mathbf{H}}\cdot\mathring{\mathbf{V}}\,dy=-\int_{\Gamma}\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{3i}\mathring{\mathbf{V}}_{i}\mathring{\mathbf{H}}\,dS+\int_{\Omega}\mathring{\mathbf{H}}(\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}\cdot\mathring{\mathbf{V}})\,dy+\int_{\Omega}\partial_{l}\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{li}\mathring{\mathbf{H}}\mathring{\mathbf{V}}_{i}\,dy,

(4.41)

where the second and the third term can be controlled in the same way as in Section
3.2
.

For the boundary term in (
4.41
), we no longer need to plug the precise form of
\mathring{\psi}
into it and find the subtle cancelltaion as in Section
3.2
because the energy estimate is not required to be
\kappa
-independent. Instead, we integrate
\overline{\partial}{}^{1/2}
by parts then apply Kato-Ponce inequality (
2.1
) and Sobolev embedding
{H}^{0.5}(\mathbb{T}^{2})\hookrightarrow L^{4}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
to get

\displaystyle-\int_{\Gamma}\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{3i}\mathring{\mathbf{V}}_{i}\mathring{\mathbf{H}}\,dS

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega}\left(\frac{\partial h}{\partial N}\right)\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\left(\Lambda_{\kappa}^{2}\mathring{\eta}_{k}\right)\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{3k}\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{3i}\mathring{\mathbf{V}}_{i}\,dS

(4.42)

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\left|\partial_{3}h\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{3k}\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{3i}\right|_{L^{\infty}}\left|\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}(\Lambda_{\kappa}^{2}\mathring{\eta}_{k})\right|_{0.5}+\left|\partial_{3}h\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{3k}\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{3i}\right|_{W^{\frac{1}{2},4}}\left|\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}(\Lambda_{\kappa}^{2}\mathring{\eta}_{k})\right|_{L^{4}}\right)|\mathring{\mathbf{V}}|_{\dot{H}^{-\frac{1}{2}}}\,dS

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{\kappa}P\left(\|h\|_{4},\|v\|_{4},\|\mathring{\eta}\|_{4}\right).

Another difference is that our flow map now contains a directional viscosity term, and thus the second term on LHS or (
4.40
) should be treated differently. We plug the expression of Alinhac good unknown
\mathring{\mathbf{V}}
and
v=\partial_{t}\eta-\mathring{\psi}
into it to get

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\left((\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\eta\right)\cdot(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\mathring{\mathbf{V}}\,dy

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\left((\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\eta\right)\cdot(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\left(\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}v-\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\mathring{\tilde{{\eta}}}\cdot\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}v\right)\,dy

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\Omega}\left|\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\left((\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\eta\right)\right|^{2}\,dy

\displaystyle-\int_{\Omega}\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\left((\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\eta\right)\cdot\left(\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\left((\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\mathring{\psi}\right)-\left[\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta},(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\right]v-(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\left(\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\mathring{\tilde{{\eta}}}\cdot\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}v\right)\right).

Direct computation shows that

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{3}\left\|\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\left((\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\eta\right)(t)\right\|_{0}^{2}\bigg|^{T}_{0}\lesssim\sum_{j=1}^{3}P\left(\|\mathbf{F}^{0},\eta,\mathring{\tilde{{\eta}}},v\|_{4},\|(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\eta,(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\mathring{\tilde{{\eta}}},(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\mathring{\psi}\|_{4}\right).

(4.43)

Summing up (
4.39
)-(
4.43
), we get the tangential estimates as follows

\left\|\overline{\partial}{}^{4}v\right\|_{0}^{2}+\sum_{j=1}^{3}\left\|\overline{\partial}{}^{4}\left((\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\eta\right)\right\|_{0}^{2}+\left\|\sqrt{e^{\prime}(\mathring{h})}\overline{\partial}{}^{4}h\right\|_{0}^{2}\lesssim\mathcal{P}_{0}+\int_{0}^{T}P(\mathring{\mathbb{E}}_{\kappa}(t))\,dt.

(4.44)

Divergence estimates:

The divergence estimate still follows the similar way as in Section
3
. We know that
\text{div}_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}v=-e^{\prime}(\mathring{h})\partial_{t}h
and thus

\|\text{div }v\|_{3}^{2}\lesssim\delta^{2}\|v\|_{4}^{2}+\|e^{\prime}(\mathring{h})\partial_{t}h\|_{3}^{2}

(4.45)

The formula of
\text{div}_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\eta
can be computed in the same way as (
3.44
)

\displaystyle\text{div}_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\eta(T)=

\displaystyle-\text{div }\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}+\int_{0}^{T}\text{div }_{\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}_{t}}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\eta\,dt+\int_{0}^{T}\text{div}_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)(v+\mathring{\psi})\,dt

(4.46)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\text{div }\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}+\int_{0}^{T}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)e^{\prime}(\mathring{h})\partial_{t}h\,dt

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{T}\text{div}_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\mathring{\psi}\underbrace{-\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{lr}\partial_{m}\partial_{t}\mathring{\tilde{{\eta}}}_{r}\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{mi}}_{=\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}_{t}^{li}}\partial_{l}((\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\eta_{i})+\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{li}\partial_{l}((\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)v_{i})\,dt

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-e^{\prime}(\mathring{h})(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)h(T)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{T}\underbrace{\text{div}_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}((\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\mathring{\psi})-\left(\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}^{r}((\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\eta_{i})\right)\left(\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}^{i}\partial_{t}\mathring{\tilde{{\eta}}}_{r}\right)+\left(\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}^{i}((\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\eta_{r})\right)\left(\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}^{r}v_{i}\right)}_{=:N_{j}}\,dt,

and thus

\left\|\text{div }(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\eta(T)\right\|_{3}^{2}\lesssim\delta^{2}\left\|(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\eta\right\|_{4}^{2}+\left\|e^{\prime}(\mathring{h})(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)h\right\|_{3}^{2}+\left\|\int_{0}^{T}N_{j}(t)\,dt\right\|_{3}^{2}

(4.47)

Therefore, the divergence control is again reduced to the weighted estimates of
\partial_{t}h
and
(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)h
.

4.3.2
Elliptic estimates and linearized wave equations

Note that both
\partial_{t}
and
(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)
are tangential derivatives on the boundary, so
h|_{\Gamma}=0
implies that
\partial_{t}h
and
(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)h
also vanish on the boundary. Therefore we can apply the elliptic estimates to both
\partial_{t}h
and
(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)h
.

We take divergence in the second equation of (
4.2
) and invoke the third equation to get the linearized wave equation

\displaystyle e^{\prime}(\mathring{h})\partial_{t}^{2}h-\Delta_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}h=

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{3}e^{\prime}(\mathring{h})(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)^{2}h-\partial_{t}\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{li}\partial_{l}v_{i}-\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}^{i}\left((\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\mathring{\tilde{{\eta}}}_{l}\right)\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}^{l}\left((\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\mathring{\tilde{{\eta}}}_{i}\right)

(4.48)

\displaystyle-e^{\prime\prime}(\mathring{h})(\partial_{t}h)(\partial_{t}\mathring{h})+\sum_{j=1}^{3}e^{\prime\prime}(\mathring{h})\left((\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)h\right)\left((\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\mathring{h}\right)-\sum_{j=1}^{3}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\int_{0}^{T}N_{j}\,dt.

Invoking Lemma
2.6
, we get

\|h\|_{4}\lesssim P(\|\mathring{\tilde{{\eta}}}\|_{3})\left(\|\Delta_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}h\|_{2}+\|\overline{\partial}{}\mathring{\tilde{{\eta}}}\|_{3}\|h\|_{3}\right).

(4.49)

Then plugging the wave equation (
4.48
), we get similar estimates as in the treatment of (
3.90
)

\|\Delta_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}h\|_{2}\lesssim\left\|e^{\prime}(\mathring{h})\partial_{t}^{2}h\right\|_{2}+\sum_{j=1}^{3}\left\|e^{\prime}(\mathring{h})(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)^{2}h\right\|_{2}+\mathcal{P}_{0}+\int_{0}^{T}P(\mathring{\mathbb{E}}_{\kappa}(t))\,dt.

(4.50)

Therefore
\|h\|_{4}
is reduced to
\left\|e^{\prime}(\mathring{h})\partial_{t}^{2}h\right\|_{2}
adn
\sum\limits_{j=1}^{3}\left\|e^{\prime}(\mathring{h})(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)^{2}h\right\|_{2}
.

Similarly,
\|\partial_{t}h\|_{3}
can be controlled by using Lemma
2.6
and
\partial_{t}
-differentiated wave equation (
4.48
)

\|\partial_{t}h\|_{3}\approx\|\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}\partial_{t}h\|_{2}\lesssim\|\partial_{t}\Delta_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}h\|_{1}+\|[\partial_{t},\Delta_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}]h\|_{1},

(4.51)

and then

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}\Delta_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}h\|_{1}\lesssim

\displaystyle\|e^{\prime}(\mathring{h})\partial_{t}^{3}h\|+1+\sum_{j=1}^{3}\left\|e^{\prime}(\mathring{h})(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)^{2}\partial_{t}h\right\|_{1}

(4.52)

\displaystyle+\|\partial_{t}(\partial_{t}\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}\partial v)\|_{1}+\sum_{j=1}^{3}\left\|\partial_{t}(\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}((\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\eta))\right\|_{1}\left\|(\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}((\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\eta))\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle+\|e^{\prime\prime}(\mathring{h})\partial_{t}\mathring{h}\partial_{t}^{2}h\|_{1}+\|e^{\prime\prime}(\mathring{h})\partial_{t}\mathring{h}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)^{2}h\|_{1}

\displaystyle+\|e^{\prime\prime\prime}(\mathring{h})\partial_{t}^{2}\mathring{h}\partial_{t}h\|_{1}+\|e^{\prime\prime\prime}(\mathring{h})\partial_{t}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)h\cdot(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)h\|_{1}+\sum_{j=1}^{3}\|(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)N_{j}\|_{1}.

Mimicing the proof of (
3.94
) yields

\|\partial_{t}\Delta_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}h\|_{1}\lesssim\|e^{\prime}(\mathring{h})\partial_{t}^{3}h\|+1+\sum_{j=1}^{3}\left\|e^{\prime}(\mathring{h})(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)^{2}\partial_{t}h\right\|_{1}+\mathcal{P}_{0}+\int_{0}^{T}P(\mathring{\mathbb{E}}_{\kappa}(t))\,dt.

(4.53)

Following the same manner of (
3.94
), (
3.95
), (
3.96
) and (
3.98
), we can get the following reduction

\displaystyle\left\|(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)h\right\|_{3}\rightarrow

\displaystyle\left\|{e^{\prime}(\mathring{h})}\partial_{t}^{2}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)h\right\|_{1}+\sum_{k=1}^{3}\left\|{e^{\prime}(\mathring{h})}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{k}\cdot\partial)^{2}\right\|_{1}

(4.54)

\displaystyle\left\|\sqrt{e^{\prime}(\mathring{h})}\partial_{t}^{2}h\right\|_{2}\rightarrow

\displaystyle\left\|{e^{\prime}(\mathring{h})}^{\frac{3}{2}}\partial_{t}^{4}h\right\|_{0}+\sum_{j=1}^{3}\left\|{e^{\prime}(\mathring{h})}^{\frac{3}{2}}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)^{2}\partial_{t}^{2}h\right\|_{0}

(4.55)

\displaystyle\left\|\sqrt{e^{\prime}(\mathring{h})}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)^{2}h\right\|_{2}\rightarrow

\displaystyle\left\|{e^{\prime}(\mathring{h})}^{\frac{3}{2}}\partial_{t}^{2}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)^{2}h\right\|_{0}+\sum_{k=1}^{3}\left\|{e^{\prime}(\mathring{h})}^{\frac{3}{2}}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)^{2}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{k}\cdot\partial)^{2}h\right\|_{0}.

(4.56)

Therefore, as in (
3.99
), it remains to control the following quantities

\displaystyle\left\|e^{\prime}(\mathring{h})\partial_{t}^{3}h\right\|_{1}+\left\|(e^{\prime}(\mathring{h}))^{\frac{3}{2}}\partial_{t}^{4}h\right\|_{0}

(4.57)

\displaystyle+\sum_{j=1}^{3}\left\|(e^{\prime}(\mathring{h}))^{2}\partial_{t}^{2}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)h\right\|_{1}+\sum_{j=1}^{3}\left\|(e^{\prime}(\mathring{h}))^{\frac{3}{2}}\partial_{t}^{2}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)^{2}h\right\|_{0}

\displaystyle+\sum_{j=1}^{3}\left\|(e^{\prime}(\mathring{h}))^{2}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)^{2}\partial_{t}^{2}h\right\|_{0}+\sum_{j=1}^{3}\left\|e^{\prime}(\mathring{h})(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)^{2}\partial_{t}h\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle+\sum_{j,k=1}^{3}\left\|(e^{\prime}(\mathring{h}))^{2}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{k}\cdot\partial)^{2}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)^{2}h\right\|_{0}+\sum_{k=1}^{3}\left\|(e^{\prime}(\mathring{h}))^{2}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{k}\cdot\partial)^{2}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)h\right\|_{1},

which will be estimated by
\partial_{t}^{3}
-differentiated,
\partial_{t}^{2}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)
-differentiated,
\partial_{t}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)^{2}
-differentiated and
\sum\limits_{k=1}^{3}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{k}\cdot\partial)^{2}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)
-differentiated wave equation as in Section
3.4
. There is no essential difference but just replacing lower order term of
h
and weight function
e^{\prime}(h)
by the counterpart of
\mathring{h}
and replacing
\tilde{a}
by
\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}
due to the linearisation. So we omit the proof here.

4.3.3
Reduction of time derivatives of
v
and
(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\eta

Finally, we follow the same manner as in (
3.101
), (
3.102
) and (
3.103
) to get the following reduction

\displaystyle\left\|e^{\prime}(\mathring{h})\partial_{t}^{4}v\right\|_{0}\lesssim

\displaystyle\|e^{\prime}(\mathring{h})\partial_{t}^{3}h\|_{1}+\sum_{j=1}^{3}\|\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\|_{L^{\infty}}\left\|e^{\prime}(\mathring{h})(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\partial_{t}^{3}\eta\right\|_{1}+\text{lower order terms}.

(4.58)

\displaystyle\left\|e^{\prime}(\mathring{h})(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\partial_{t}^{4}\eta\right\|_{0}\lesssim

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\|_{L^{\infty}}\left\|e^{\prime}(\mathring{h})\partial_{t}^{3}v\right\|_{1}+\left\|e^{\prime}(\mathring{h})(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\partial_{t}^{3}\mathring{\psi}\right\|_{0}.

\displaystyle\left\|(e^{\prime}(\mathring{h}))^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{t}^{3}v\right\|_{1}\lesssim

\displaystyle\left\|(e^{\prime}(\mathring{h}))^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}\partial_{t}^{2}h\right\|_{1}+\sum_{j=1}^{3}\|\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\|_{2}\left\|(e^{\prime}(\mathring{h}))^{\frac{1}{2}}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\partial_{t}^{2}\eta\right\|_{2}+\text{lower order terms}.

\displaystyle\left\|(e^{\prime}(\mathring{h}))^{\frac{1}{2}}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\partial_{t}^{3}\eta\right\|_{1}\lesssim

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\|_{2}\left\|(e^{\prime}(\mathring{h}))^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{t}^{2}v\right\|_{2}+\left\|(e^{\prime}(\mathring{h}))^{\frac{1}{2}}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\partial_{t}^{2}\mathring{\psi}\right\|_{1}.

\displaystyle\left\|\partial_{t}^{2}v\right\|_{2}\lesssim

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}\partial_{t}h\|_{2}+\sum_{j=1}^{3}\|\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\|_{2}\|(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\partial_{t}\eta\|_{3}+\text{lower order terms}.

\displaystyle\left\|(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\partial_{t}^{2}\eta\right\|_{2}\lesssim

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\|_{2}\|\partial_{t}v\|_{3}+\|(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\partial_{t}\mathring{\psi}\|_{2}.

\displaystyle\left\|\partial_{t}v\right\|_{3}\lesssim

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}h\|_{3}+\sum_{j=1}^{3}\|\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\|_{3}\|(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\eta\|_{4}.

\displaystyle\left\|(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\partial_{t}\eta\right\|_{3}\lesssim

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\|_{3}\|v\|_{4}+\|(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\mathring{\psi}\|_{4}.

Combining (
4.33
), (
4.44
), (
4.45
), (
4.47
), (
4.49
), (
4.53
)-(
4.58
), we get

\mathring{\mathbb{E}}_{\kappa}(T)\lesssim_{\kappa^{-1}}\mathcal{P}_{0}+P(\mathring{\mathbb{E}}_{\kappa}(T))\int_{0}^{T}P(\mathring{\mathbb{E}}_{\kappa}(t))\,dt

(4.59)

and thus by Gronwall-type inequality, there exists
T_{\kappa}>0
such that the following uniform-in-
n
estimates hold

\sup_{0\leq t\leq T_{\kappa}}\mathring{\mathbb{E}}_{\kappa}(t)\lesssim P(\|v_{0}\|_{4}^{,}\|\mathfrak{h}_{0}\|_{4},\|\mathbf{F}^{0}\|_{4}).

(4.60)

Therefore Proposition
4.2
is proven.

4.4
Picard iteration to the nonlinear approximation system

The last step is to construct the solution to (
3.2
) by Picard iteration which proves Propostion
4.3
. Define

[v]^{(n)}:=v^{(n+1)}-v^{(n)},~~[h]^{(n)}:=h^{(n+1)}-h^{(n)},~~[\eta]^{(n)}:=\eta^{(n+1)}-\eta^{(n)},

(4.61)

and

[a]^{(n)}:=a^{(n)}-a^{(n-1)},~~[\psi]^{(n)}:=\psi^{(n)}-\psi^{(n-1)}.

(4.62)

Then we have the following system of
([\eta]^{(n)},[v]^{(n)},[h]^{(n)})

\begin{cases}\partial_{t}[\eta]^{(n)}=[v]^{(n)}+[\psi]^{(n)}~~~&\text{ in }\Omega\\
\partial_{t}[v]^{(n)}=-\nabla_{\tilde{a}^{(n)}}[h]^{(n)}-\nabla_{[\tilde{a}]^{(n)}}h^{(n)}+\sum\limits_{j=1}^{3}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)^{2}[\eta]^{(n)}~~~&\text{ in }\Omega\\
\text{div}_{\tilde{a}^{(n)}}[v]^{(n)}=-e^{\prime}(h^{(n)})\partial_{t}[h]^{(n)}-\text{div}_{[\tilde{a}]^{(n)}}v^{(n)}-(e^{\prime}(h^{(n)})-e^{\prime}(h^{(n-1)}))\partial_{t}h^{(n)}~~~&\text{ in }\Omega\\
\text{div }\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}=-e^{\prime}(\mathfrak{h}_{0})(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\mathfrak{h}_{0}~~~&\text{ in }\Omega\\
[h]^{(n)}=0,\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot N=0~~~&\text{ on }\Gamma,\\
\end{cases}

(4.63)

together with its energy functional

\displaystyle{}^{(n)}(T)

\displaystyle:=\left\|[\eta]^{(n)}\right\|_{3}^{2}+\left\|[v]^{(n)}\right\|_{3}^{2}+\sum\limits_{j=1}^{3}\left\|(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)[\eta]^{(n)}\right\|_{3}^{2}+\left\|[h]^{(n)}\right\|_{3}^{2}

(4.64)

\displaystyle+\left\|\partial_{t}[v]^{(n)}\right\|_{2}^{2}+\sum\limits_{j=1}^{3}\left\|(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\partial_{t}[\eta]^{(n)}\right\|_{2}^{2}+\left\|\partial_{t}[h]^{(n)}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle+\left\|\partial_{t}^{2}[v]^{(n)}\right\|_{1}^{2}+\sum\limits_{j=1}^{3}\left\|(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\partial_{t}^{2}\eta^{(n)}\right\|_{1}^{2}+\left\|\sqrt{e^{\prime}(h^{(n)})}\partial_{t}^{2}[h]^{(n)}\right\|_{1}^{2}

\displaystyle+\left\|\sqrt{e^{\prime}(h^{(n)})}\partial_{t}^{3}[v]^{(n)}\right\|_{0}^{2}+\sum\limits_{j=1}^{3}\left\|\sqrt{e^{\prime}(h^{(n)})}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\partial_{t}^{3}[\eta]^{(n)}\right\|_{0}^{2}+\left\|e^{\prime}(h^{(n)})\partial_{t}^{3}[h]^{(n)}\right\|_{0}^{2}.

Here the correction term
[\psi]^{(n)}
becomes

-\Delta[\psi]^{(n)}=0,

(4.65)

with the following boundary condition

\displaystyle[\psi]^{(n)}=

\displaystyle\sum_{L=1}^{2}\overline{\partial}{}^{-1}\mathbb{P}\bigg(\overline{\Delta}{}[\eta]^{(n-1)}_{k}\tilde{a}^{(n)Lk}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\Lambda_{\kappa}^{2}v^{(n)}+\overline{\partial}{}\eta^{(n-1)}_{\beta}[\tilde{a}]^{(n)Lk}\overline{\partial}{}_{L}\Lambda_{\kappa}^{2}v^{(n)}+\overline{\partial}{}\eta^{(n-1)}_{k}\tilde{a}^{(n-1)Lk}\overline{\partial}{}_{L}\Lambda_{\kappa}^{2}[v]^{(n-1)}

\displaystyle-\overline{\Delta}{}\Lambda_{\kappa}^{2}[\eta]^{(n-1)}_{k}\tilde{a}^{(n)Lk}\overline{\partial}{}_{L}v^{(n)}-\overline{\Delta}{}\Lambda_{\kappa}^{2}\eta^{(n-1)}_{k}[\tilde{a}]^{(n)Lk}\overline{\partial}{}_{L}v^{(n)}-\overline{\Delta}{}\Lambda_{\kappa}^{2}\eta^{(n-1)}_{k}\tilde{a}^{(n-1)Lk}\overline{\partial}{}_{L}[v]^{(n-1)}\bigg).

4.4.1
Estimates of
[\eta],[\tilde{a}],[\psi]

First by definition we have

\displaystyle[a]^{(n)li}(T)=\int_{0}^{T}\partial_{t}(a^{(n)li}-a^{(n-1)li})\,dt=-\int_{0}^{T}[a]^{(n)lr}\partial_{k}\partial_{t}\eta^{(n)}_{r}a^{(n)ki}+a^{(n-1)lr}\partial_{k}\partial_{t}[\eta]^{(n-1)}_{r}a^{(n)ki}+a^{(n-1)lr}\partial_{k}\partial_{t}\eta^{(n-1)}_{r}[a]^{(n)ki},

and thus

\|[a]^{(n)}(T)\|_{2}\lesssim\int_{0}^{T}\|[a]^{(n)}(T)\|_{2}^{2}(\|[v]^{(n-1)}\|_{3}+\|[\psi]^{(n)}\|_{3})\,dt.

(4.66)

The estimates of
[\psi]^{(n)}
and
(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)[\psi]^{(n)}
can be similarly derived by elliptic estimates

\|[\psi]^{(n)}\|_{3}^{2}\lesssim|[\psi]^{(n)}|_{2.5}\lesssim\|[\eta]^{(n-1)}\|_{3}^{2}+\|[v]^{(n-1)}\|_{2}^{2}+\|[\tilde{a}]^{(n)}\|_{1}^{2},

(4.67)

and

\|(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)[\psi]^{(n)}\|_{3}^{2}\lesssim|[\psi]^{(n)}|_{2.5}\lesssim P\left(\|(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)[\eta]^{(n-1)}\|_{3},\|[\eta]^{(n-1)}\|_{3},\|[v]^{(n-1)}\|_{2},\|[\tilde{a}]^{(n)}\|_{1}\right).

(4.68)

Analogous results hold for their time derivatives so we omit the proof

\displaystyle[\psi]^{(n)},\partial_{t}[\psi]^{(n)},(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)[\psi]^{(n)},\partial_{t}[\eta]^{(n)},(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)[\eta]^{(n)}\in

\displaystyle L^{\infty}([0,T];H^{3}(\Omega)),

(4.69)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}^{2}[\psi]^{(n)},\partial_{t}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)[\psi]^{(n)},\partial_{t}^{2}[\eta]^{(n)},\partial_{t}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)[\eta]^{(n)}\in

\displaystyle L^{\infty}([0,T];H^{2}(\Omega)),

(4.70)

\displaystyle\sqrt{e^{\prime}(h^{(n)})}\partial_{t}^{3}[\psi]^{(n)},\partial_{t}^{2}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)[\psi]^{(n)},\partial_{t}^{3}[\eta]^{(n)},\partial_{t}^{2}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)[\eta]^{(n)}\in

\displaystyle L^{\infty}([0,T];H^{1}(\Omega)),

(4.71)

\displaystyle e^{\prime}(h^{(n)})\partial_{t}^{4}[\psi]^{(n)},\sqrt{e^{\prime}(h^{(n)})}\left(\partial_{t}^{3}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)[\psi]^{(n)},\partial_{t}^{4}[\eta]^{(n)},\partial_{t}^{3}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)[\eta]^{(n)}\right)\in

\displaystyle L^{\infty}([0,T];L^{2}(\Omega)).

(4.72)

4.4.2
Curl estimates of
[v]
and
(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)[\eta]

Similarly as in Section
3.1
, we get the evolution equation of
\text{curl }_{\tilde{a}^{(n)}}[v]^{(n)}
as

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\left(\text{curl }_{\tilde{a}^{(n)}}[v]^{(n)}\right)-\sum_{j=1}^{3}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\left(\text{curl }_{\tilde{a}^{(n)}}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)[\eta]^{(n)}\right)

(4.73)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\text{curl }_{\tilde{a}_{t}^{(n)}}[v]^{(n)}+\text{curl }_{[\tilde{a}_{t}]^{(n)}}v^{(n)}+\sum_{j=1}^{3}\left[\text{curl }_{\tilde{a}^{(n)}},(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\right]((\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)[\eta]^{(n)})+(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\left(\text{curl }_{[\tilde{a}]^{(n)}}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\eta^{(n)}\right)+\left[\text{curl }_{[\tilde{a}]^{(n)}},(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\right]((\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\eta^{(n)}).

Then the curl estimates directly follows from the
L^{2}
-estimates of
\partial^{2}
-differentiated evolution equation.

4.4.3
Tangential estimates of
[v]
and
(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)[\eta]

We adopt the Ainhac good unknown method as in Section
3.2
. For each
n
we define

\mathbf{V}^{(n+1)}=\overline{\partial}{}^{3}v^{(n+1)}-\overline{\partial}{}^{3}\tilde{\eta}^{(n)}\cdot\nabla_{\tilde{a}^{(n)}}v^{(n+1)},~~\mathbf{H}^{(n+1)}=\overline{\partial}{}^{3}h^{(n+1)}-\overline{\partial}{}^{3}\tilde{\eta}^{(n)}\cdot\nabla_{\tilde{a}^{(n)}}h^{(n+1)}.

Their differences are denoted by

[\mathbf{V}]^{(n)}:=\mathbf{V}^{(n+1)}-\mathbf{V}^{(n)},[\mathbf{H}]^{(n)}:=\mathbf{H}^{(n+1)}-\mathbf{H}^{(n)}.

The evolution equation of
[\mathbf{V}]
and
[\mathbf{H}]
now becomes

\displaystyle\partial_{t}[\mathbf{V}]^{(n)}+\nabla_{\tilde{a}^{(n)}}[\mathbf{H}]^{(n)}-\sum_{j=1}^{3}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\overline{\partial}{}^{3}((\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)[\eta]^{(n)})=-\nabla_{[\tilde{a}]^{(n)}}\mathbf{H}^{(n)}+\mathfrak{f}^{(n)},

(4.74)

with boundary condition

[\mathbf{H}]^{(n)}|_{\Gamma}=-\left(\overline{\partial}{}^{3}\tilde{\eta}^{(n)}_{k}\tilde{a}^{(n)3k}+\overline{\partial}{}^{3}[\tilde{\eta}]^{(n-1)}_{k}\tilde{a}^{(n)3k}+\overline{\partial}{}^{3}\tilde{\eta}^{(n-1)}_{k}[\tilde{a}]^{(n)3k}\right),

(4.75)

and the compressibility equation

\nabla_{\tilde{a}^{(n)}}\cdot[\mathbf{V}]^{(n)}=-\nabla_{[\tilde{a}]^{(n)}}\cdot\mathbf{V}^{(n)}+\mathfrak{g}^{(n)}.

(4.76)

Here

\displaystyle\mathfrak{f}^{(n)i}

\displaystyle=\partial_{t}\left(\overline{\partial}{}^{3}[\tilde{\eta}]^{(n-1)}_{k}\tilde{a}^{(n)lk}\partial_{l}v^{(n+1)}_{i}+\overline{\partial}{}^{3}\tilde{\eta}^{(n-1)}_{k}[\tilde{a}]^{(n)lk}\partial_{\mu}v^{(n+1)}_{i}+\overline{\partial}{}^{3}\tilde{\eta}^{(n-1)}_{k}\tilde{a}^{(n)lk}\partial_{l}[v]^{(n)}_{i}\right)

\displaystyle~~~~+[\tilde{a}]^{(n)lk}\partial_{l}(\tilde{a}^{(n)ri}\partial_{r}h^{(n+1)})\overline{\partial}{}^{3}\tilde{\eta}^{(n)}_{k}+\tilde{a}^{(n-1)lk}\partial_{l}([\tilde{a}]^{(n)ri}\partial_{r}h^{(n+1)})\overline{\partial}{}^{3}\tilde{\eta}^{(n)}_{k}

\displaystyle~~~~+\tilde{a}^{(n-1)lk}\partial_{l}(\tilde{a}^{(n-1)ri}\partial_{r}[h]^{(n)})\overline{\partial}{}^{3}\tilde{\eta}^{(n)}_{k}+\tilde{a}^{(n-1)lk}\partial_{l}([\tilde{a}]^{(n)ri}\partial_{r}h^{(n)})\overline{\partial}{}^{3}[\tilde{\eta}]^{(n-1)}_{k}

\displaystyle~~~~-\left[\overline{\partial}{}^{2},[\tilde{a}]^{(n)lk}\tilde{a}^{(n)ri}\overline{\partial}{}\right]\partial_{r}\tilde{\eta}^{(n)}_{k}\partial_{l}h^{(n+1)}-\left[\overline{\partial}{}^{2},\tilde{a}^{(n-1)lk}[\tilde{a}]^{(n)ri}\overline{\partial}{}\right]\partial_{r}\tilde{\eta}^{(n)}_{k}\partial_{l}h^{(n+1)}

\displaystyle~~~~-\left[\overline{\partial}{}^{2},\tilde{a}^{(n-1)lk}\tilde{a}^{(n-1)ri}\overline{\partial}{}\right]\partial_{r}[\tilde{\eta}]^{(n-1)}_{k}\partial_{l}h^{(n+1)}-\left[\overline{\partial}{}^{2},\tilde{a}^{(n-1)lk}\tilde{a}^{(n-1)ri}\overline{\partial}{}\right]\partial_{r}\tilde{\eta}^{(n-1)}_{k}\partial_{l}[h]^{(n)}

\displaystyle~~~~-\left[\overline{\partial}{}^{3},[\tilde{a}]^{(n)li},\partial_{l}h^{(n+1)}\right]-\left[\overline{\partial}{}^{3},\tilde{a}^{(n-1)li},\partial_{l}[h]^{(n)}\right]+\sum_{j=1}^{3}\left[\overline{\partial}{}^{3}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\right](\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)[\eta]^{(n)},

and

\displaystyle\mathfrak{g}^{(n)}

\displaystyle=\overline{\partial}{}^{3}(\text{div }_{\tilde{a}^{(n)}}[v]^{(n)}-\text{div }_{[\tilde{a}]^{(n)}}v^{(n)})

\displaystyle~~~~-\left[\overline{\partial}{}^{2},[\tilde{a}]^{(n)lk}\tilde{a}^{(n)ri}\overline{\partial}{}\right]\partial_{r}\tilde{\eta}^{(n)}_{k}\partial_{l}v^{(n+1)}_{i}-\left[\overline{\partial}{}^{2},\tilde{a}^{(n-1)lk}[\tilde{a}]^{(n)ri}\overline{\partial}{}\right]\partial_{r}\tilde{\eta}^{(n)}_{k}\partial_{l}v^{(n+1)}_{i}

\displaystyle~~~~-\left[\overline{\partial}{}^{2},\tilde{a}^{(n-1)lk}\tilde{a}^{(n-1)ri}\overline{\partial}{}\right]\partial_{r}[\tilde{\eta}]^{(n-1)}_{k}\partial_{l}v^{(n+1)}_{i}-\left[\overline{\partial}{}^{2},\tilde{a}^{(n-1)lk}\tilde{a}^{(n)ri}\overline{\partial}{}\right]\partial_{r}\tilde{\eta}^{(n-1)}_{k}\partial_{l}[v]^{(n)}_{i}

\displaystyle~~~~-\left[\overline{\partial}{}^{3},[\tilde{a}]^{(n)li},\partial_{l}v^{(n+1)}_{i}\right]-\left[\overline{\partial}{}^{3},\tilde{a}^{(n-1)li},\partial_{l}[v]^{(n)}_{i}\right]

\displaystyle~~~~+[\tilde{a}]^{(n)lk}\partial_{l}(\tilde{a}^{(n)ri}\partial_{r}v^{(n+1)}_{i})\overline{\partial}{}^{3}\tilde{\eta}^{(n)}_{k}+\tilde{a}^{(n-1)lk}\partial_{l}([\tilde{a}]^{(n)ri}\partial_{r}v^{(n+1)}_{i})\overline{\partial}{}^{3}\tilde{\eta}^{(n)}_{k}

\displaystyle~~~~+\tilde{a}^{(n-1)lk}\partial_{l}(\tilde{a}^{(n-1)ri}\partial_{r}[v]^{(n)}_{i})\overline{\partial}{}^{3}\tilde{\eta}^{(n)}_{k}+\tilde{a}^{(n-1)lk}\partial_{l}([\tilde{a}]^{(n)ri}\partial_{r}v^{(n)}_{i})\overline{\partial}{}^{3}[\tilde{\eta}]^{(n-1)}_{k}.

Then we do the
L^{2}
-estimates of the good unknowns to get analogous results as in (
4.44
)

\left\|\overline{\partial}{}^{3}[v]^{(n)}\right\|_{0}^{2}+\sum_{j=1}^{3}\left\|\overline{\partial}{}^{3}\left((\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)[\eta]^{(n)}\right)\right\|_{0}^{2}+\left\|\sqrt{e^{\prime}(h^{(n)})}\overline{\partial}{}^{3}[h]^{(n)}\right\|_{0}^{2}\lesssim_{\kappa^{-1}}\mathcal{P}_{0}+\int_{0}^{T}[\mathbb{E}]^{(n)}(t)+\mathbb{E}^{(n-1)}(t)\,dt.

(4.77)

4.4.4
Divergence estimates and Reduction procedure

Similarly as in (
4.46
), we derive the formula of
\text{div }_{\tilde{a}^{(n)}}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)[\eta]^{(n+1)}

\displaystyle\text{div }_{\tilde{a}^{(n)}}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)[\eta]^{(n+1)}=-e^{\prime}(h^{(n)})(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)[h]^{(n)}+\text{div }_{[a]^{(n)}}\eta^{(n)}-(e^{\prime}(h^{(n)})-e^{\prime}(h^{(n-1)}))(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)h^{(n)}

(4.78)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\Bigg(\text{div }_{\tilde{a}^{(n)}}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)[\psi]^{(n)}+\text{div }_{[\tilde{a}]^{(n-1)}}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\psi^{(n-1)}

\displaystyle-\left(\nabla_{\tilde{a}^{(n)}}^{r}((\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)[\eta_{i}]^{(n)})+\nabla_{[\tilde{a}]^{(n)}}^{r}((\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\eta_{i}^{(n)})+\nabla_{\tilde{a}^{(n-1)}}^{r}((\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\eta_{i}^{(n)})\right)

\displaystyle\cdot\left(\nabla_{\tilde{a}^{(n)}}^{r}([\partial_{t}\eta_{i}]^{(n)})+\nabla_{[\tilde{a}]^{(n)}}^{r}(\partial_{t}\eta_{i}^{(n)})+\nabla_{\tilde{a}^{(n-1)}}^{r}(\partial_{t}\eta_{i}^{(n)})\right)

\displaystyle-\left(\nabla_{\tilde{a}^{(n)}}^{r}((\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)[\eta_{i}]^{(n)})+\nabla_{[\tilde{a}]^{(n)}}^{r}((\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\eta_{i}^{(n)})+\nabla_{\tilde{a}^{(n-1)}}^{r}((\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\eta_{i}^{(n)})\right)

\displaystyle\cdot\left(\nabla_{\tilde{a}^{(n)}}^{r}[v_{i}]^{(n)}+\nabla_{[\tilde{a}]^{(n)}}^{r}v_{i}^{(n)}+\nabla_{\tilde{a}^{(n-1)}}^{r}v_{i}^{(n)}\right)\Bigg)\,dt.

The wave equation of
[h]^{(n+1)}
becomes

\displaystyle e^{\prime}(h^{(n)})\partial_{t}^{2}[h]^{(n)}-\Delta_{\tilde{a}^{(n)}}[h]^{(n)}=-\sum_{j=1}^{3}e^{\prime}(h^{(n)})(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)^{2}[h]^{(n)}

(4.79)

\displaystyle-\partial_{t}\left((e^{\prime}(h^{(n)})-e^{\prime}(h^{(n-1)}))\partial_{t}h^{(n)}\right)-\sum_{j=1}^{3}(e^{\prime}(h^{(n)})-e^{\prime}(h^{(n-1)}))(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)^{2}h^{(n)}

\displaystyle-(\partial_{t}\tilde{a}^{(n)li})\partial_{l}[v]^{(n)}_{i}-\partial_{t}[\tilde{a}]^{(n)li}\partial_{l}v^{(n)}_{i}+[\tilde{a}]^{(n)li}\partial_{l}({\tilde{a}^{(n)m}}_{i}\partial_{m}h^{(n)})+\tilde{a}^{(n-1)li}\partial_{l}({[\tilde{a}]^{(n)m}}_{i}\partial_{m}h^{(n)})

\displaystyle-\sum_{j=1}^{3}\left(\nabla_{\tilde{a}^{(n)}}^{i}((\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)[\tilde{\eta}_{l}]^{(n)})+\nabla_{[\tilde{a}]^{(n)}}^{i}((\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\tilde{\eta}_{l}^{(n)})+\nabla_{\tilde{a}^{(n-1)}}^{i}((\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\tilde{\eta}_{l}^{(n)})\right)

\displaystyle\cdot\left(\nabla_{\tilde{a}^{(n)}}^{l}((\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)[\tilde{\eta}_{i}]^{(n)})+\nabla_{[\tilde{a}]^{(n)}}^{l}((\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\tilde{\eta}_{i}^{(n)})+\nabla_{\tilde{a}^{(n-1)}}^{l}((\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\tilde{\eta}_{i}^{(n)})\right)

\displaystyle+(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\left(\text{div }_{[a]^{(n)}}\eta^{(n)}-(e^{\prime}(h^{(n)})-e^{\prime}(h^{(n-1)}))(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)h^{(n)}\right)+(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\int_{0}^{T}\cdots\,dt,

where the time integral is the same as in (
4.78
). Similarly as the previous reduction procedure shown in (
4.54
)-(
4.56
) we are able to finalize the energy estimates. We omit the details here because there is no essential difference from the previous control for the linearized equation.

Finally, we can apply Gronwall-type inequality to prove that there exists a sufficiently small
T_{\kappa}>0
such that

\forall t\in[0,T_{\kappa}],~~~[\mathbb{E}]^{(n)}\leq\frac{1}{4}\left([\mathbb{E}]^{(n-1)}+[\mathbb{E}]^{(n-2)}\right),

and thus

[\mathbb{E}]^{(n)}\lesssim_{\kappa^{-1}}\mathcal{P}_{0}/2^{n}.

Therefore, the sequence of approximation solutions
\{(\eta^{(n)},v^{(n)},h^{(n)})\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
strongly converges (subsequentially) to
(\eta(\kappa),v(\kappa),h(\kappa))
of (
3.2
) as
n\to\infty
. The local well-posedness of the nonlinear
\kappa
-approximation system is established.

5
Local well-posedness of the original system

Now we can finalize the proof of local well-posedness of the free-boundary compressible elastodynamic equations (
1.14
). In Proposition
4.3
, we proved that for each fixed
\kappa>0
, the nonlinear
\kappa
-approximation problem admits a unique strong solution
(\eta(\kappa),v(\kappa),h(\kappa))
in time interval
[0,T_{\kappa}]
. In Proposition
3.1
, we derive the uniform-in-
\kappa
a priori estimates for
(\eta(\kappa),v(\kappa),h(\kappa))
. Therefore, there exists a
T>0
independent of
\kappa
, such that
(\eta(\kappa),v(\kappa),h(\kappa))
exists in
[0,T]
for each
\kappa>0
. Also, such uniform energy bound yields the strong convergence (subsequentially) of
(\eta(\kappa),v(\kappa),h(\kappa))
to a limit
(\eta,v,h)
in
[0,T]
which solves the original system (
1.14
) and the corresponding energy functional
\mathbb{E}(T)
defined in (
1.15
) satisfies the energy bound (
1.17
).

It remains to prove the uniqueness of the solution. We suppose
(\eta^{1},v^{1},h^{1}),(\eta^{2},v^{2},h^{2})
to be two solutions to (
1.14
) which satisfies the energy estimate (
1.17
) in Theorem
1.1
. Denote the difference by
([\eta],[v],[h]):=(\eta^{1}-\eta^{2},v^{1}-v^{2},h^{1}-h^{2})
and
a^{L}:=[\partial\eta^{L}]^{-1}
(
L=1,2
) with
[a]:=a^{2}-a^{1}
. Then
([\eta],[v],[h])
solves the following system
with ZERO initial data
:

\begin{cases}\partial_{t}[\eta]=[v]~~~&\text{ in }\Omega,\\
\partial_{t}[v]+\sum\limits_{j=1}^{3}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)^{2}[\eta]=-\nabla_{a^{1}}[h]+\nabla_{[a]}h^{2}~~~&\text{in }\Omega,\\
\text{div }_{a^{1}}[v]=\text{div }_{[a]}v^{2}-e^{\prime}(h^{2})\partial_{t}[h]-(e^{\prime}(h^{1})-e^{\prime}(h^{2}))\partial_{t}h^{2}~~~&\text{in }\Omega\\
[h]=0~~~&\text{ on }\Gamma.\end{cases}

(5.1)

We define the energy functional of (
5.1
) by

\displaystyle=
