\displaystyle\left\|[\eta]\right\|_{2}^{2}+\sum_{k=0}^{2}\left\|\partial_{t}^{2-k}[v]\right\|_{k}^{2}+\sum_{j=1}^{3}\left\|\partial_{t}^{2-k}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)[\eta]\right\|_{k}^{2}+\left|(a^{1})^{3i}\overline{\partial}{}^{2}[i]_{i}\right|_{0}^{2}

(5.2)

\displaystyle+\|[h]\|_{2}+\|\partial_{t}[h]\|_{2}+\left\|\sqrt{e^{\prime}(h^{1})}\partial_{t}^{2}[h]\right\|_{0}^{2}

The only essential difference in the energy estimates is the boundary integral

\int_{\Gamma}[\mathbf{H}](a^{1})^{3i}[\mathbf{V}]_{i}\,dS.

.

We define the Alinhac good unknowns of
v^{L},h^{L}
for
L=1,2

\mathbf{V}^{L}=\overline{\partial}{}^{2}v^{L}-\overline{\partial}{}^{2}\eta^{L}\cdot\nabla_{a^{L}}v^{L},~~\mathbf{H}^{L}=\overline{\partial}{}^{2}h^{L}-\overline{\partial}{}^{2}\eta^{L}\cdot\nabla_{a^{L}}h^{L},

and

[\mathbf{V}]:=\mathbf{V}^{1}-\mathbf{V}^{2},~~[\mathbf{H}]:=\mathbf{H}^{1}-\mathbf{H}^{2}.

The boundary integral then becomes

\displaystyle\int_{\Gamma}[\mathbf{H}](a^{1})^{3i}[\mathbf{V}]_{i}=

\displaystyle-\int_{\Gamma}\partial_{3}[h]\overline{\partial}{}^{2}\eta^{2}_{k}(a^{2})^{3k}(a^{2})^{3i}[\mathbf{V}]_{i}\,dS-\int_{\Gamma}\partial_{3}h^{1}(\overline{\partial}{}^{2}[\eta]_{k}(a^{1})^{3k}+\overline{\partial}{}^{2}\eta^{2}_{k}[a]^{3k})(a^{1})^{3i}[\mathbf{V}]_{i}\,dS

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\Gamma}\partial_{3}h^{1}|(a^{1})^{3i}\overline{\partial}{}^{2}[\eta]_{i}|_{0}^{2}\,dS

\displaystyle-\int_{\Gamma}\partial_{3}h^{1}(a^{1})^{3r}\overline{\partial}{}^{2}[\eta]_{r}(\overline{\partial}{}^{2}\eta^{2}_{k}[a]^{lk}\partial_{l}v^{1}_{i}-\overline{\partial}{}^{2}\eta^{2}_{k}(a^{2})^{lk}\partial_{\mu}[v]_{i})(a^{1})^{3i}\,dS

\displaystyle-\int_{\Gamma}\partial_{3}h^{1}(\overline{\partial}{}^{2}[\eta]_{k}(a^{1})^{3k}+\overline{\partial}{}^{2}\eta^{2}_{k}[a]^{3k})(a^{1})^{3i}[\mathbf{V}]_{i}\,dS

\displaystyle\lesssim-\frac{c_{0}}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\Gamma}|(a^{1})^{3i}\overline{\partial}{}^{2}[\eta]_{i}|_{0}^{2}\,dS+P(\text{initial data})P([\mathbb{E}](t)).

Here we use the precise formula of
[\mathbf{V}]
, and in the third step we apply the physical sign condition for
h^{1}
. Therefore we have

\sup_{t\in[0,T_{0}]}[\mathbb{E}](t)\leq P(\text{initial data})+\int_{0}^{T_{0}}P([\mathbb{E}](t))\,dt.

Since the initial data of (
5.1
) is 0, then we know
[\mathbb{E}](t)=0
for all
t\in[0,T]
which gives the uniqueness of the solution to the compressible elastodynamics system (
1.14
). Theorem
1.1
is proven.

6
Incompressible limit

In this section we will prove Theorem
1.2
, i.e., the incompressible limit. This requires our energy estimate to be uniform in the sound speed. In physics, the sound speed of a compressible fluid is defined by
c^{2}(t,x):=p^{\prime}(\rho)
. We parametrize the sound speed by
\varepsilon>0
such that

{p^{\prime}}_{\varepsilon}(\rho)|_{\rho=1}=\varepsilon.

Recall that the enthalpy derivative
{\mathfrak{h}^{\prime}}_{\varepsilon}(\rho):=\frac{{p^{\prime}}_{\varepsilon}(\rho)}{\rho}>0
. We can write
\rho
as a function of
\mathfrak{h}
depending on
\varepsilon
.

In the proof of Theorem
1.1
, the estimate of the weighted energy functional
\mathbb{E}(T)
is uniform in sound speed
\varepsilon
, i.e., it does not rely on
1/e^{\prime}(h)
. Once we have this uniformly bounded energy, we are able to prove that the solution
(v^{\varepsilon},\mathbf{F}^{\varepsilon},h^{\varepsilon})
of (
1.11
) converges to
(V,G,Q)
of incompressible elastodynamics system (
1.18
) provided that the initial data
(v_{0}^{\varepsilon},\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\varepsilon},\mathfrak{h}_{0}^{\varepsilon})
converges to the initial data
(\textbf{v}_{0},\mathbf{G}_{0},Q_{0})
as
\varepsilon\to\infty
.

Specifically, let
\textbf{v}_{0}
be a divergence-free vector field,
\mathbf{G}^{0}
be a divergence-free matrix and
Q_{0}
is defined by the elliptic system with constraints
-\frac{\partial Q}{\partial N}|_{\Gamma}\geq c_{0}>0

\begin{cases}-\Delta Q_{0}=\partial_{i}\textbf{v}_{0}^{k}\partial_{k}\textbf{v}_{0}^{i}-\partial_{i}\mathbf{G}^{0}_{kj}\partial_{k}\mathbf{G}^{0}_{ij}~~~&\text{in }\Omega,\\
Q_{0}=0~~~&\text{on }\Gamma.\\
\end{cases}

Let
(V,G,Q)
be the solution to the free-boundary incompressible elastodynamic system (
1.18
) with initial data
(\textbf{v}_{0},\mathbf{G}^{0},Q_{0})\in H^{4}\times H^{4}\times H^{4}
:

\begin{cases}\partial_{t}\eta=V~~~&\text{in }\Omega,\\
\partial_{t}V=-\nabla_{a}Q+\sum\limits_{j=1}^{3}(G\cdot\nabla_{a})G~~~&\text{in }\Omega,\\
\text{div }_{a}V=0~~~&\text{in }\Omega,\\
(\text{div }G^{\top})_{j}:=\partial_{k}G_{kj}=0~~~&\text{in }\Omega,\\
\partial_{t}|_{\Gamma}\in\mathcal{T}([0,T]\times\Gamma)~~~&\text{on }\Gamma,\\
Q=0~~~&\text{on }\Gamma,\\
G_{j}\cdot N=0~~~&\text{on }\Gamma,\\
-\frac{\partial Q}{\partial N}\geq c_{0}>0~~~&\text{on }\Gamma,\\
(\eta,V,G,Q)|_{t=0}=(\text{Id},\textbf{v}_{0},\mathbf{G}^{0},Q_{0}).\end{cases}

Suppose that there exists initial data
(v_{0}^{\varepsilon},\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\varepsilon},\mathfrak{h}_{0}^{\varepsilon})
of compressible elastodynamic system (
1.11
) converging to
(\textbf{v}_{0},\mathbf{G}_{0},Q_{0})
in
C^{1}
-norm (proved in the Section
8
). Then the uniform-in-
\varepsilon
energy estimates (
1.17
) show that
v^{\varepsilon},h^{\varepsilon},F^{\varepsilon}
are all
C_{t,x}^{1}
are uniformly bounded and also that
\partial_{t}v,\partial_{t}F,\partial_{t}h\in C^{0,\frac{1}{2}}
by Morrey’s embedding. Therefore, we actually show that
v^{\varepsilon},h^{\varepsilon},F^{\varepsilon}
are equi-continuous in
C_{t,x}^{1}
, and thus
(v^{\varepsilon},F^{\varepsilon},h^{\varepsilon})
has a convergent subsequence by Arzelà-Ascoli Lemma. As
\varepsilon\to+\infty
, we have
e^{\prime}(h_{\varepsilon})\partial_{t}h_{\varepsilon},~e^{\prime}(h_{\varepsilon})(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)h_{\varepsilon}\to 0
which implies

(v^{\varepsilon},F^{\varepsilon},h^{\varepsilon})\to(V,G,Q)

provided the convergence of initial data. Finally, we have

\rho_{\varepsilon}(h)\to 1\text{ and }e_{\varepsilon}(h)\to 0,\text{ as }\varepsilon\to\infty.

(6.1)

Therefore the incompressible limit is established. Theorem
1.2
is proven.

7
Enhanced regularity of full time derivatives

In Lindblad-Luo
[
60
]
and Luo
[
62
]
concerned with compressible Euler equations, their energy functionals contain the
H^{1}
-norm of
\partial_{t}^{4}
-derivative to close the energy estimates and also the incompressible limit. This is because they had to include the full time derivatives in the boundary energy. In the presenting manuscript we no longer need those terms because we exactly have proven that the boundary energy is contributed by the full spatial tangential derivatives. Our result is also applicable to compressible Euler equations. Yet we are still able to prove the higher regularity of full time derivatives of
(v,F,h)
to recover the previous incompressible limit results for free-boundary Euler equations when the elastic medium is slightly compressible, i.e.,
|e^{\prime}(h)|\ll 1
is sufficiently small.

However, we cannot mimic the proof in
[
60
,
62
]
to derive analogous result for compressible elastodynamic equations. The essential reason is that the source term of the following wave equation loses one derivative in
\sum\limits_{j=1}^{3}e^{\prime}(h)(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)^{2}h
due to the appearance of the deformation tensor
F

\displaystyle e^{\prime}(h)\partial_{t}^{2}h-\Delta_{a}h=

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{3}e^{\prime}(h)(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)^{2}h-\partial_{t}a^{li}\partial_{l}v_{i}-\sum_{j=1}^{3}(\nabla_{a}^{i}F_{lj})\cdot(\nabla_{a}^{l}F_{ij})-e^{\prime\prime}(h)(\partial_{t}h)^{2}+\sum_{j=1}^{3}e^{\prime\prime}(h)((\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)h)^{2}.

(7.1)

If we analyze the
\partial_{t}^{4}
-differentiated wave equation (
7.1
), then we will have to control
\partial\partial_{t}^{4}v
coming from the source term. In
[
60
,
62
]
, one can invoke the Euler equations
\partial_{t}v=-\nabla_{a}h
to replace
\partial\partial_{t}^{4}v
by
\partial^{2}\partial_{t}^{3}h
whose
L^{2}
-norm can be reduced to
\|\partial_{t}^{5}h\|_{0}
plus lower order terms by Lemma
2.6
. However, the presence of deformation tensor produces another term
\partial((\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial))\partial_{t}^{3}F
. Since
F\neq\mathbf{O}
on the boundary, one cannot apply Lemma
2.6
to
F
and thus has to analyze higher order spatial derivatives which will ruin the whole proof.

We notice that, for elastodynamic equations (
1.11
), we can do further div-curl-tangential control and delicate analysis of Alinhac good unknowns and the
\partial_{t}^{4}
-differentiated wave equation to estimate the weighted norms of
\partial_{t}^{4}v,\partial_{t}^{4}F
and
\partial_{t}^{4}h,\partial_{t}^{5}h
. The loss of derivative caused by
e^{\prime}(h)(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)^{2}h
can be absorbed if the compressibility of the elastic medium is sufficiently slight. Thus, our proof is totally different from
[
60
,
62
,
91
]
.

7.1
Higher order wave equations and divergence control

From now on, we again have
\mathbf{F}_{j}=(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\eta
. Taking
\partial_{t}^{4}
in (
7.1
) yields

\displaystyle e^{\prime}(h)\partial_{t}^{6}h-\text{div }_{a}(\partial_{t}^{4}\nabla_{a}h)=

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{3}e^{\prime}(h)(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)^{2}\partial_{t}^{4}h+\underbrace{\partial_{t}^{4}\left((\nabla_{a}^{i}v_{l})(\nabla_{a}^{l}v_{i})-\sum_{j=1}^{3}(\nabla_{a}^{i}F_{lj})\cdot(\nabla_{a}^{l}F_{ij})\right)}_{Z_{1}}

(7.2)

\displaystyle\underbrace{-\partial_{t}^{4}\left(e^{\prime\prime}(h)(\partial_{t}h)^{2}\right)+\sum_{j=1}^{3}\partial_{t}^{4}\left(e^{\prime\prime}(h)((\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)h)^{2}\right)}_{Z_{2}}

\displaystyle\underbrace{-\left[\partial_{t}^{4},e^{\prime}(h)\right]\partial_{t}^{2}h+\left[\partial_{t}^{4},\text{div }_{a}\right]\nabla_{a}h}_{Z_{3}}.

We compute the
L^{2}
inner product of (
7.2
) and
(e^{\prime}(h))^{2}\partial_{t}^{5}h
to get

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\Omega}\left|(e^{\prime}(h))^{\frac{3}{2}}\partial_{t}^{5}h\right|^{2}+\left|e^{\prime}(h)\partial_{t}^{4}\nabla_{a}h\right|^{2}\,dy

(7.3)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{3}\int_{\Omega}(e^{\prime}(h))^{3}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)^{2}\partial_{t}^{4}h\cdot\partial_{t}^{5}h\,dy+\int_{\Omega}(e^{\prime}(h))^{2}(Z_{1}+Z_{2}+Z_{3})\partial_{t}^{5}h\,dy

(7.4)

\displaystyle+\frac{3}{2}\int_{\Omega}(e^{\prime}(h))^{2}e^{\prime\prime}(h)\partial_{t}h\left|\partial_{t}^{5}h\right|^{2}\,dy+\int_{\Omega}e^{\prime}(h)e^{\prime\prime}(h)\partial_{t}h\left|\partial_{t}^{4}\nabla_{a}h\right|^{2}\,dy

(7.5)

\displaystyle+\int_{\Omega}(e^{\prime}(h))^{2}\left[\nabla_{a},\partial_{t}^{4}\right]\partial_{t}h\,dy+2e^{\prime}(h)e^{\prime\prime}(h)(\nabla_{a}h)\partial_{t}^{5}h(\partial_{t}^{4}\nabla_{a}h)\,dy

(7.6)

Let us analyze each term in details. In the first term of (
7.4
), we can integrate
(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)
by parts to get the divergence control of
\mathbf{F}

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}(e^{\prime}(h))^{3}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)^{2}\partial_{t}^{4}h\cdot\partial_{t}^{5}h\,dy

(7.7)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\Omega}\left|(e^{\prime}(h))^{\frac{3}{2}}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\partial_{t}^{4}h\right|^{2}\,dy+\frac{3}{2}\int_{\Omega}(e^{\prime}(h))^{2}e^{\prime\prime}(h)\partial_{t}h\left|(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\partial_{t}^{4}h\right|^{2}\,dy

\displaystyle-3\int_{\Omega}(e^{\prime}(h))^{2}e^{\prime\prime}(h)((\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)h)\left((\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\partial_{t}^{4}h\right)\partial_{t}^{5}h\,dy-\int_{\Omega}(e^{\prime}(h))^{3}(\text{div }\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j})\left((\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\partial_{t}^{4}h\right)\partial_{t}^{5}h\,dy

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\Omega}\left|(e^{\prime}(h))^{\frac{3}{2}}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\partial_{t}^{4}h\right|^{2}\,dy+\|e^{\prime}(h)\partial_{t}h\|_{L^{\infty}}\left\|(e^{\prime}(h))^{\frac{3}{2}}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\partial_{t}^{4}h\right\|_{0}^{2}

\displaystyle+\left(\left\|e^{\prime}(h)(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)h\right\|_{L^{\infty}}+\left\|\text{div }\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}\right)\left\|(e^{\prime}(h))^{\frac{3}{2}}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\partial_{t}^{4}h\right\|_{0}\left\|(e^{\prime}(h))^{\frac{3}{2}}\partial_{t}^{5}h\right\|_{0}

The analysis of the second term in (
7.4
) will be postponed to the end of this part. Now we analyze (
7.5
).

\displaystyle\frac{3}{2}\int_{\Omega}(e^{\prime}(h))^{2}e^{\prime\prime}(h)\partial_{t}h\left|\partial_{t}^{5}h\right|^{2}\,dy+\int_{\Omega}e^{\prime}(h)e^{\prime\prime}(h)\partial_{t}h\left|\partial_{t}^{4}\nabla_{a}h\right|^{2}\,dy

(7.8)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\|e^{\prime}(h)\partial_{t}h\|_{L^{\infty}}\left(\left\|(e^{\prime}(h))^{\frac{3}{2}}\partial_{t}^{5}h\right\|_{0}^{2}+\left\|e^{\prime}(h)\partial_{t}^{4}\nabla_{a}h\right\|_{0}^{2}\right)

Then we analyze (
7.6
) term by term (we omit the coefficients)

\displaystyle-\left[\nabla_{a},\partial_{t}^{4}\right]\partial_{t}h=\partial_{t}^{4}a\cdot\partial\partial_{t}h+\partial_{t}^{3}a\cdot\partial\partial_{t}^{2}h+\partial_{t}^{2}a\cdot\partial\partial_{t}^{3}h+\partial_{t}a\cdot\partial\partial_{t}^{4}h,

and thus

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}(e^{\prime}(h))^{2}\left[\nabla_{a},\partial_{t}^{4}\right]\partial_{t}h\,dy

(7.9)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\left\|\sqrt{e^{\prime}(h)}\partial_{t}^{3}v\right\|_{1}\left\|\partial_{t}h\right\|_{1}\|e^{\prime}(h)^{\frac{3}{2}}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\partial_{t}^{2}v\|_{1}\|e^{\prime}(h)\partial_{t}^{2}h\|_{1}\|e^{\prime}(h)\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle+\|\partial_{t}v\|_{1}\cdot\|e^{\prime}(h)\partial_{t}^{3}h\|_{1}\|e^{\prime}(h)\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\partial v\cdot a\|_{1}\|e^{\prime}(h)\nabla_{a}\partial_{t}^{4}h\|_{0}\|e^{\prime}(h)\|_{L^{\infty}}

So (
7.6
) is estimated as follows

\displaystyle\eqref{h5z5}\lesssim

\displaystyle\left\|\sqrt{e^{\prime}(h)}\partial_{t}^{3}v\right\|_{1}\left\|\partial_{t}h\right\|_{1}\|e^{\prime}(h)^{\frac{3}{2}}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\partial_{t}^{2}v\|_{1}\|e^{\prime}(h)\partial_{t}^{2}h\|_{1}\|e^{\prime}(h)\|_{L^{\infty}}

(7.10)

\displaystyle+\|\partial_{t}v\|_{1}\cdot\|e^{\prime}(h)\partial_{t}^{3}h\|_{1}\|e^{\prime}(h)\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\partial v\cdot a\|_{1}\|e^{\prime}(h)\nabla_{a}\partial_{t}^{4}h\|_{0}\|e^{\prime}(h)\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle+\left\|\sqrt{e^{\prime}(h)}\nabla_{a}h\right\|_{L^{\infty}}\left\|e^{\prime}(h)\partial_{t}^{4}\nabla_{a}h\right\|_{0}\left\|(e^{\prime}(h))^{\frac{3}{2}}\partial_{t}^{5}h\right\|_{0}

Finally, we analyze the second integral in (
7.4
). In fact, it suffices to compute
\left\|\sqrt{e^{\prime}(h)}(Z_{1}+Z_{2}+Z_{3})\right\|_{0}
. We first write
Z_{1}
term by term,

\displaystyle Z_{1}=

\displaystyle 2\partial_{t}^{4}(\nabla_{a}v)\cdot(\nabla_{a}v)-2\partial_{t}^{4}(\nabla_{a}F)\cdot(\nabla_{a}F)

\displaystyle+8\partial_{t}^{3}(\nabla_{a}v)\cdot\partial_{t}(\nabla_{a}v)-8\partial_{t}^{3}(\nabla_{a}F)\cdot\partial_{t}(\nabla_{a}F)

\displaystyle+6\partial_{t}^{2}(\nabla_{a}v)\cdot\partial_{t}^{2}(\nabla_{a}v)-6\partial_{t}^{2}(\nabla_{a}F)\cdot\partial_{t}^{2}(\nabla_{a}F),

and thus

\displaystyle\left\|\sqrt{e^{\prime}(h)}Z_{1}\right\|_{0}\lesssim P(\|\eta\|_{4})

\displaystyle\bigg(\left\|\sqrt{e^{\prime}(h)}\partial_{t}^{4}v\right\|_{1}\|\partial v\|_{L^{\infty}}+\left\|\sqrt{e^{\prime}(h)}\partial_{t}^{4}F\right\|_{1}\|\partial F\|_{L^{\infty}}

(7.11)

\displaystyle+\left\|\sqrt{e^{\prime}(h)}\partial_{t}^{3}v\right\|_{1}\|\partial\partial_{t}v\|_{L^{\infty}}+\left\|\sqrt{e^{\prime}(h)}\partial_{t}^{3}F\right\|_{1}\|\partial\partial_{t}F\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle+\left\|\sqrt{e^{\prime}(h)}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}\left(\left\|\partial_{t}^{2}v\right\|_{2}^{2}+\left\|\partial_{t}^{2}F\right\|_{2}^{2}\right)\bigg).

For
Z_{2}
, it suffices to analyze its first term because
\partial_{t}h
and
(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)h
always have the same power of weight functions. We have

\displaystyle\sqrt{e^{\prime}(h)}

\displaystyle\partial_{t}^{4}\left(e^{\prime\prime}(h)(\partial_{t}h)^{2}\right)=\sqrt{e^{\prime}(h)}e^{\prime\prime}(h)\left(2\partial_{t}^{5}h\partial_{t}h+8\partial_{t}^{4}h\partial_{t}^{2}h+12\partial_{t}^{3}h\partial_{t}^{3}h\right)

\displaystyle+\sqrt{e^{\prime}(h)}e^{(3)}(h)\partial_{t}h\left(2\partial_{t}^{4}h\partial_{t}h+6\partial_{t}^{3}h\partial_{t}^{2}h\right)

\displaystyle+\sqrt{e^{\prime}(h)}\left(e^{(4)}(h)(\partial_{t}h)^{2}+e^{(3)}(h)\partial_{t}^{2}h\right)\left(2\partial_{t}^{3}h\partial_{t}h+4\partial_{t}^{2}h\partial_{t}^{2}h\right)

\displaystyle+\sqrt{e^{\prime}(h)}\left(e^{(5)}(h)(\partial_{t}h)^{3}+3e^{(4)}(h)\partial_{t}h\partial_{t}^{2}h+e^{(3)}(h)\partial_{t}^{3}h\right)\left(2\partial_{t}^{2}h\partial_{t}h\right)

\displaystyle+\sqrt{e^{\prime}(h)}\left(e^{(6)}(h)(\partial_{t}h)^{4}+6e^{(5)}(h)\partial_{t}^{2}h(\partial_{t}h)^{2}+3e^{(4)}(h)(\partial_{t}^{2}h\partial_{t}^{2}h+\partial_{t}h\partial_{t}^{3}h)+e^{(3)}(h)\partial_{t}^{4}h\right)\left(\partial_{t}h\right)^{2}

Invoking the physical constraints (
1.9
), we know

\displaystyle\left\|\sqrt{e^{\prime}(h)}\partial_{t}^{4}\left(e^{\prime\prime}(h)(\partial_{t}h)^{2}\right)\right\|_{0}

(7.12)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle P\left(\left\|\sqrt{e^{\prime}(h)}\right\|_{L^{\infty}},\left\|(e^{\prime}(h))^{\frac{3}{2}}\partial_{t}^{5}h\right\|_{0},\left\|(e^{\prime}(h))^{\frac{3}{2}}\partial_{t}^{4}h\right\|_{0},\left\|e^{\prime}(h)\partial_{t}^{3}h\right\|_{1},\left\|(e^{\prime}(h))\partial_{t}^{2}h\right\|_{2},\left\|\partial_{t}h\right\|_{2}\right)

Similar result holds for the second term by replacing
\partial_{t}
by
(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)
so we omit the details.

For
Z_{3}
, we have (omitting the coefficients)

\displaystyle Z_{3}:=

\displaystyle-\left[\partial_{t}^{4},e^{\prime}(h)\right]\partial_{t}^{2}h+\left[\partial_{t}^{4},\text{div }_{a}\right]\nabla_{a}h

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-e^{\prime\prime}(h)(\partial_{t}h\partial_{t}^{5}h+\partial_{t}^{2}h\partial_{t}^{4}h+\partial_{t}^{3}h\partial_{t}^{3}h)-e^{(3)}(h)((\partial_{t}h)^{2}\partial_{t}^{4}h+\partial_{t}h\partial_{t}^{2}\partial_{t}^{3}h+(\partial_{t}^{2}h)^{3})

\displaystyle-e^{(4)}(h)((\partial_{t}h)^{3}\partial_{t}^{3}h+(\partial_{t}h)^{2}(\partial_{t}^{2}h)^{2})-e^{(5)}(h)(\partial_{t}h)^{4}\partial_{t}^{2}h

\displaystyle+\partial_{t}^{4}a\cdot\partial(\nabla_{a}h)+\partial_{t}^{3}a\cdot\partial\partial_{t}(\nabla_{a}h)+\partial_{t}^{2}a\cdot\partial\partial_{t}^{2}(\nabla_{a}h)+\partial_{t}a\cdot\partial\partial_{t}^{3}(\nabla_{a}h).

Therefore

\displaystyle\left\|\sqrt{e^{\prime}(h)}Z_{3}\right\|_{0}

(7.13)

\displaystyle\lesssim P\bigg(

\displaystyle\|e^{\prime}(h)\|_{L^{\infty}},\left\|(e^{\prime}(h))^{\frac{3}{2}}\partial_{t}^{5}h\right\|_{0},\left\|(e^{\prime}(h))^{\frac{3}{2}}\partial_{t}^{4}h\right\|_{0},\left\|e^{\prime}(h)\partial_{t}^{3}h\right\|_{1},\left\|(e^{\prime}(h))\partial_{t}^{2}h\right\|_{2},\left\|\partial_{t}h\right\|_{3},

\displaystyle\|h\|_{4},\left\|\sqrt{e^{\prime}(h)}\partial_{t}^{3}v\right\|_{1},\|\partial_{t}^{2}v\|_{2},\|\partial_{t}v\|_{2}\bigg)+\|\partial v\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\partial\eta\|_{L^{\infty}}\left\|\sqrt{e^{\prime}(h)}\partial_{t}^{3}\nabla_{a}h\right\|_{1}

In the last term we again invoke Lemma
2.6
to get

\displaystyle\left\|\sqrt{e^{\prime}(h)}\partial_{t}^{3}\nabla_{a}h\right\|_{1}\leq

\displaystyle\left\|\sqrt{e^{\prime}(h)}\nabla_{a}\partial_{t}^{3}h\right\|_{1}+\left\|\sqrt{e^{\prime}(h)}[\partial_{t}^{3},\nabla_{a}]h\right\|_{1}

(7.14)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\left\|\sqrt{e^{\prime}(h)}\Delta_{a}\partial_{t}^{3}h\right\|_{0}+\left\|\sqrt{e^{\prime}(h)}[\partial_{t}^{3},\nabla_{a}]h\right\|_{1}+L.O.T.

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left\|\sqrt{e^{\prime}(h)}\partial_{t}^{3}\Delta_{a}h\right\|_{0}+\left\|\sqrt{e^{\prime}(h)}[\partial_{t}^{3},\Delta_{a}]\right\|_{0}+\left\|\sqrt{e^{\prime}(h)}[\partial_{t}^{3},\nabla_{a}]h\right\|_{1}+L.O.T.

Invoking the
\partial_{t}^{3}
-differentiated wave equation (
3.105
), we know the two commutators above has been controlled in the analysis of (
3.105
) so we do not repeat here. The only term that we have to be careful is the first source term in (
3.105
), i.e.,
\sum\limits_{j=1}^{3}e^{\prime}(h)(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)^{2}\partial_{t}^{3}h
. The inequality (
7.14
) now becomes

\left\|\sqrt{e^{\prime}(h)}\partial_{t}^{3}\nabla_{a}h\right\|_{1}\lesssim\sum_{j=1}^{3}\left\|\sqrt{e^{\prime}(h)}e^{\prime}(h)(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)^{2}\partial_{t}^{3}h\right\|_{0}\lesssim\|\mathbf{F}^{0}\|_{2}^{2}\|e^{\prime}(h)\|_{L^{\infty}}\left\|\sqrt{e^{\prime}(h)}\partial_{t}^{3}h\right\|_{2}

(7.15)

Since
\left\|\sqrt{e^{\prime}(h)}\partial_{t}^{3}\nabla_{a}h\right\|_{1}\approx\left\|\sqrt{e^{\prime}(h)}\partial_{t}^{3}h\right\|_{2}
and
|e^{\prime}(h)|\ll 1
for a slightly compressible elastic medium, the RHS can be absorbed to LHS by letting
\varepsilon
sufficiently large (or say
e^{\prime}(h)
sufficiently small).

Thus, the energy of
\partial_{t}^{4}
-differentiated wave equation is controlled as follows

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\left(\int_{\Omega}\left|(e^{\prime}(h))^{\frac{3}{2}}\partial_{t}^{5}h\right|^{2}+\left|e^{\prime}(h)\partial_{t}^{4}\nabla_{a}h\right|^{2}+\sum_{j=1}^{3}\left|(e^{\prime}(h))^{\frac{3}{2}}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\partial_{t}^{4}h\right|^{2}\,dy\right)\lesssim P(\mathfrak{E}(T)).

(7.16)

7.2
Curl control

According to Lemma
2.5
, we need to control the
L^{2}
-norm of divergence, curl, and tangential derivative of
\partial_{t}^{4}v
and
\partial_{t}^{4}\mathbf{F}
. In Section
7.1
, we control the weighted divergence of
v
and
F
because

\sqrt{e^{\prime}(h)}\text{div }\partial_{t}^{4}h\approx-(e^{\prime}(h))^{\frac{3}{2}}\partial_{t}^{5}h,~~\sqrt{e^{\prime}(h)}\text{div }\partial_{t}^{4}F_{j}\approx\sqrt{e^{\prime}(h)}\text{div }_{a}\partial_{t}^{4}((\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\eta)=-(e^{\prime}(h))^{\frac{3}{2}}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\partial_{t}^{4}h.

This part we are going to control the curl. Keep in mind
F_{j}=(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\eta
.

We take
\partial_{t}^{4}\text{curl }_{a}
in the seond equation of (
1.14
) to get the evolution equation of curl

\partial_{t}^{4}(\text{curl }_{a}\partial_{t}v)=\sum_{j=1}^{3}\partial_{t}^{4}\left(\text{curl }_{a}((\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)F_{j})\right).

and thus

\displaystyle\partial_{t}(\text{curl }_{a}\partial_{t}^{4}v)-\sum_{j=1}^{3}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\text{curl }_{a}\partial_{t}^{4}F_{j}=

\displaystyle-\left[(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial),\text{curl }_{a}\right]\partial_{t}^{4}F_{j}

(7.17)

\displaystyle-3\text{curl }_{\partial_{t}a}\partial_{t}^{4}v-6\text{curl }_{\partial_{t}^{2}a}\partial_{t}^{3}v

\displaystyle-4\text{curl }_{\partial_{t}^{3}a}\partial_{t}^{2}v-\text{curl }_{\partial_{t}^{4}a}\partial_{t}v

\displaystyle+\sum_{j=1}^{3}\bigg(4\text{curl }_{\partial_{t}a}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\partial_{t}^{3}F_{j}+6\text{curl }_{\partial_{t}^{2}a}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\partial_{t}^{2}F_{j}

\displaystyle+4\text{curl }_{\partial_{t}^{3}a}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\partial_{t}F_{j}+\text{curl }_{\partial_{t}^{4}a}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)F_{j}\bigg).

Then we compute the
L^{2}
inner product of (
7.17
) and
e^{\prime}(h)\text{curl }_{a}\partial_{t}^{4}v
to get

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\Omega}e^{\prime}(h)\left|\text{curl }_{a}\partial_{t}^{4}v\right|^{2}+\sum_{j=1}^{3}e^{\prime}(h)\left|\text{curl }_{a}\partial_{t}^{4}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\eta\right|^{2}\,dy

(7.18)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{3}\int_{\Omega}e^{\prime}(h)\left(\text{curl }_{a}\partial_{t}^{4}v\right)\cdot\left[(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial),\text{curl }_{a}\right]\partial_{t}^{4}v\,dy

\displaystyle+\text{RHS of }\eqref{curl5eq}+\cdots,

where the omitted terms correspond to the case that dertivatives fall on the weight function (which must be lower order and of higher power of weight functions).

There is only one term that cannot be controlled by direct computation:
4\sum\limits_{j=1}^{3}\text{curl }_{\partial_{t}a}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\partial_{t}^{3}F_{j}
which requires
\|\partial_{t}^{3}F\|_{2}
. However, we invoke again the second equation of (
1.14
) to replace
\sum\limits_{j=1}^{3}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)F_{j}
by
\partial_{t}v-\nabla_{a}h
:

4\sum\limits_{j=1}^{3}\text{curl }_{\partial_{t}a}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\partial_{t}^{3}F_{j}=4\text{curl }_{\partial_{t}a}\partial_{t}^{4}v-4\text{curl }_{\partial_{t}a}\partial_{t}^{3}\nabla_{a}h,

where the first term can be directly controlled by
\|\partial_{t}a\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\partial_{t}^{4}v\|_{1}
. The second term requires the control of
\|\partial_{t}^{3}h\|_{2}
. This can again be reduced to
\partial_{t}^{5}h
by Lemma
2.6
. One can mimic the proof in (
7.15
) to control that.

Therefore, the following curl estimate holds

\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\Omega}e^{\prime}(h)\left|\text{curl }_{a}\partial_{t}^{4}v\right|^{2}+\sum_{j=1}^{3}e^{\prime}(h)\left|\text{curl }_{a}\partial_{t}^{4}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\eta\right|^{2}\,dy\lesssim P(\mathfrak{E}(T)).

(7.19)

7.3
Tangential estimates: Alinhac good unknown

It remains to do the tangential estimates. Although
\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{4}
contains time derivative, we cannot directly commute it with
\nabla_{a}
because this will produce a term like
\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{4}a=\partial_{t}^{3}\partial^{2}v\times\partial\eta+\cdots
which cannot be controlled in
L^{2}
. To avoid such problem, we again use Alinhac good unknown method. Define the Alinhac good unknowns for
v,h
with respect to
\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{4}
by

\displaystyle\mathfrak{V}:=

\displaystyle\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{4}v-\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{4}\eta\cdot\nabla_{a}v=\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{4}v-\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{3}v\cdot\nabla_{a}v,

(7.20)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{H}:=

\displaystyle\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{4}h-\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{4}\eta\cdot\nabla_{a}h=\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{4}h-\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{3}v\cdot\nabla_{a}h.

Similarly as in Section
3.2
, we have the following identity for the Alinhac good unknown
\mathfrak{g}:=\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{4}g-\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{4}\eta\cdot\nabla_{a}g
for a function
g
with respect to
\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{4}
:

\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{4}(\nabla_{a}g)=\nabla_{a}\mathfrak{g}+\mathfrak{C}(g),

(7.21)

where

\displaystyle\mathfrak{C}^{i}(g):=

\displaystyle\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{4}\eta_{r}\nabla_{a}^{i}(\nabla_{a}^{r}g)-\left(\left[\partial_{t}^{4},a^{lr}a^{mi}\right]\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{m}\eta_{r}\right)\partial_{l}f+\left[\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{4},a^{li},\partial_{l}f\right].

(7.22)

Taking
e^{\prime}(h)\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{4}
in the second equation of (
1.14
) yields that

e^{\prime}(h)\partial_{t}\mathfrak{V}-e^{\prime}(h)\nabla_{a}\mathfrak{H}=e^{\prime}(h)\sum_{j=1}^{3}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{4}F_{j}+\underbrace{e^{\prime}(h)\left(\partial_{t}(\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{3}v\cdot\nabla_{a}v)-\mathfrak{C}(h)+\left[\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{4},(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\right]F_{j}\right)}_{\mathfrak{f}}.

(7.23)

Taking
L^{2}
inner product of (
7.23
) and
\mathfrak{V}
yields

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\Omega}e^{\prime}(h)|\mathfrak{V}|^{2}\,dy=\int_{\Omega}e^{\prime}(h)(\nabla_{a}\mathfrak{H})\cdot\mathfrak{V}\,dy+\sum_{j=1}^{3}\int_{\Omega}e^{\prime}(h)(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{4}F_{j}\cdot\mathfrak{V}\,dy+\underbrace{\int_{\Omega}\mathfrak{f}\cdot\mathfrak{V}\,dy}_{\mathfrak{K}_{0}},

(7.24)

subjected to

\displaystyle\nabla_{a}\cdot\mathfrak{V}=\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{4}(\text{div }_{a}v)-\mathfrak{C}^{i}(v_{i}),

(7.25)

and

\displaystyle\mathfrak{H}|_{\Gamma}=(-\partial_{3}h)a^{3k}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{4}\eta_{k}.

(7.26)

Here in (
7.24
) all the terms containing derivatives that fall on the weight function are again omitted.

We analyze the second term on RHS of (
7.24
) by integrating
(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)
by parts

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{3}\int_{\Omega}e^{\prime}(h)(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{4}F_{j}\cdot\mathfrak{V}\,dy

(7.27)

\displaystyle\overset{(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)}{=}

\displaystyle-\sum_{j=1}^{3}\int_{\Omega}e^{\prime}(h)\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{4}\mathbf{F}_{j}\cdot(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\left(\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{4}v-\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{4}\eta\cdot\nabla_{a}v\right)+\sum_{j=1}^{3}\int_{\Omega}e^{\prime}(h)(\text{div }\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j})\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{4}F_{j}\cdot\mathfrak{V}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\sum_{j=1}^{3}\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\Omega}e^{\prime}(h)\left|\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{4}\left((\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\eta\right)\right|^{2}\,dy-\int_{\Omega}e^{\prime}(h)\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{4}F_{j}\cdot\left[(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial),\overline{\partial}{}\right]\partial_{t}^{4}v\,dy

\displaystyle-\int_{\Omega}e^{\prime}(h)\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{4}F_{j}\cdot\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{4}\left((\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\eta\right)\cdot\nabla_{a}v\,dy-\int_{\Omega}e^{\prime}(h)\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{4}F_{j}\cdot\left(\left(\left[(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial),\overline{\partial}{}\right]\partial_{t}^{4}\eta\right)\cdot\nabla_{a}v\right)

\displaystyle-\int_{\Omega}e^{\prime}(h)\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{4}F_{j}\cdot\left(\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{4}\eta\cdot\left((\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\nabla_{a}v\right)\right)\,dy+\int_{\Omega}e^{\prime}(h)(\text{div }\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j})\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{4}F_{j}\cdot\mathfrak{V}\,dy

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle-\sum_{j=1}^{3}\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\left\|\sqrt{e^{\prime}(h)}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{4}\left((\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\eta\right)\right\|_{0}^{2}+P\left(\|v\|_{4},\|\sqrt{e^{\prime}(h)}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{4}v\|_{0},\|\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{4}F\|_{0},\|\sqrt{e^{\prime}(h)}\partial_{t}^{3}v\|_{1}\right).

Next we analyze the first term in RHS of (
7.24
) which will produce boundary energy. First we integrate by parts to get

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}e^{\prime}(h)(\nabla_{a}\mathfrak{H})\cdot\mathfrak{V}\,dy

(7.28)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\int_{\Omega}e^{\prime}(h)\mathfrak{H}(\nabla_{a}\cdot\mathfrak{V})\,dy-\int_{\Omega}e^{\prime}(h)\partial_{l}a^{li}\mathfrak{H}\mathfrak{V}_{i}\,dy\underbrace{+\int_{\Gamma}e^{\prime}(h)\frac{\partial h}{\partial N}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{4}\eta_{k}a^{3k}a^{3i}\mathfrak{V}_{i}\,dS}_{=:\mathfrak{B}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\int_{\Omega}e^{\prime}(h)\mathfrak{H}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{4}(\text{div }_{a}v)\,dy+\int_{\Omega}e^{\prime}(h)\mathfrak{H}\mathfrak{C}^{i}(v_{i})\,dy-\int_{\Omega}e^{\prime}(h)\partial_{l}a^{li}\mathfrak{H}\mathfrak{V}_{i}\,dy+\mathfrak{B}

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle\mathfrak{K}_{1}+\mathfrak{K}_{2}+\mathfrak{K}_{3}+\mathfrak{B}.

We have that

\mathfrak{K}_{3}\lesssim\|\partial a\|_{L^{\infty}}\left\|\sqrt{e^{\prime}(h)}\mathfrak{H}\right\|_{0}\left\|\sqrt{e^{\prime}(h)}\mathfrak{V}\right\|_{0}.

(7.29)

Next we plug the expression of
\mathfrak{H}
into
\mathfrak{K}_{1}
to get

\displaystyle\mathfrak{K}_{1}=

\displaystyle-\int_{\Omega}e^{\prime}(h)\mathfrak{H}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{4}(\text{div }_{a}v)\,dy=\int_{\Omega}(e^{\prime}(h))^{2}(\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{4}h-\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{4}\eta\cdot\nabla_{a}h)\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{5}h\,dy

(7.30)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\left\|\sqrt{e^{\prime}(h)}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{4}h\right\|_{0}^{2}-\underbrace{\int_{\Omega}(e^{\prime}(h))^{2}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{3}v_{k}a^{lk}\partial_{l}h\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{5}h\,dy}_{\mathfrak{K}_{4}}.

Then
\mathfrak{K}_{4}
can be controlled after integrating
\partial_{t}
by parts under time integral

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\mathfrak{K}_{4}(t)\,dt:=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}(e^{\prime}(h))^{2}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{3}v_{k}a^{lk}\partial_{l}h\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{5}h\,dy\,dt

(7.31)

\displaystyle\overset{\partial_{t}}{=}

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}(e^{\prime}(h))^{2}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{4}v_{k}a^{lk}\partial_{l}h\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{4}h\,dy\,dt+\int_{\Omega}(e^{\prime}(h))^{2}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{3}v_{k}a^{lk}\partial_{l}h\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{4}h\,dy\bigg|^{T}_{0}

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}(e^{\prime}(h))^{2}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{3}v_{k}\partial_{t}(\nabla_{a}h)^{k}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{4}h\,dy

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}P\left(\|\eta\|_{3},\|h\|_{4},\|\partial_{t}h\|_{3},\|\partial_{t}^{3}v\|_{1},\left\|\sqrt{e^{\prime}(h)}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{4}v\right\|_{0},\left\|(e^{\prime}(h))^{\frac{3}{2}}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{4}h\right\|_{0}\right)\,dt

\displaystyle+\mathcal{P}_{0}+\delta\left\|e^{\prime}(h)\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{4}h\right\|_{0}^{2}+\left\|\sqrt{e^{\prime}(h)}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}\left\|\sqrt{e^{\prime}(h)}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{3}v\right\|_{0}^{2}\|\nabla_{a}h\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}.

Here in the last step we use Young’s inequality such that the
\delta
-term can be absorbed, and the last term can also be absorbed by
\mathbb{E}(T)
since
|e^{\prime}(h)|\ll 1
is assumed now. Therefore the term
\mathfrak{K}_{1}
has been controlled.

Now we come to analyze the boundary integral. We follow the same method as in Section
3.2
. Since we do not have tangential smoothing now (and thus
\psi=0
), we can find the cancellation structure in the boundary integral.

\displaystyle\mathfrak{B}:=

\displaystyle\int_{\Gamma}e^{\prime}(h)\left(\frac{\partial h}{\partial N}\right)\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{4}\eta_{k}a^{3k}a^{3i}\mathfrak{V}_{i}\,dS

(7.32)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\int_{\Gamma}e^{\prime}(h)\left(-\frac{\partial h}{\partial N}\right)\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{4}\eta_{k}a^{3k}a^{3i}\left(\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{5}\eta_{i}-\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{4}\eta\cdot\nabla_{a}v_{i}\right)\,dS

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\Gamma}\left(-\frac{\partial h}{\partial N}\right)\left|\sqrt{e^{\prime}(h)}a^{3i}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{4}\eta_{i}\right|^{2}\,dS+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Gamma}\partial_{t}\left(-\frac{\partial h}{\partial N}\right)\left|\sqrt{e^{\prime}(h)}a^{3i}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{4}\eta_{i}\right|^{2}\,dS

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\int_{\Gamma}e^{\prime}(h)\left(-\frac{\partial h}{\partial N}\right)\partial_{t}a^{3i}a^{3j}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{4}\eta_{j}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{4}\eta_{i}\,dS}_{\mathfrak{B}_{1}}+\underbrace{\int_{\Gamma}e^{\prime}(h)\left(-\frac{\partial h}{\partial N}\right)\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{4}\eta_{j}a^{3j}a^{3i}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{4}\eta_{k}a^{lk}\partial_{l}v_{i}\,dS}_{\mathfrak{B}_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle-\frac{c_{0}}{4}\frac{d}{dt}\left|\sqrt{e^{\prime}(h)}a^{3i}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{4}\eta_{i}\right|_{0}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\|\partial_{t}h\|_{3}\left|\sqrt{e^{\prime}(h)}a^{3i}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{4}\eta_{i}\right|_{0}^{2}+\mathfrak{B}_{1}+\mathfrak{B}_{2}.

Plugging
\partial_{t}a^{3i}=-a^{3r}\partial_{l}v_{r}a^{li}
into
\mathfrak{B}_{1}
yields that

\displaystyle\mathfrak{B}_{1}=

\displaystyle-\int_{\Gamma}e^{\prime}(h)\left(-\frac{\partial h}{\partial N}\right)a^{3r}\partial_{l}v_{r}a^{ri}a^{3j}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{4}\eta_{j}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{4}\eta_{i}\,dS

(7.33)

\displaystyle=-

\displaystyle\int_{\Gamma}e^{\prime}(h)\left(-\frac{\partial h}{\partial N}\right)a^{3r}\partial_{3}v_{r}a^{3i}a^{3j}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{4}\eta_{j}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{4}\eta_{i}\,dS

\displaystyle\underbrace{-\sum_{L=1}^{2}\int_{\Gamma}e^{\prime}(h)\left(-\frac{\partial h}{\partial N}\right)a^{3r}\partial_{L}v_{r}a^{Li}a^{3j}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{4}\eta_{j}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{4}\eta_{i}\,dS}_{\mathfrak{B}_{3}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle|\partial_{3}ha^{3r}\partial_{3}v_{r}|_{L^{\infty}}\left|\sqrt{e^{\prime}(h)}a^{3i}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{4}\eta_{i}\right|_{0}^{2}+\mathfrak{B}_{3}.

Next we compare
\mathfrak{B}_{2}
with
\mathfrak{B}_{3}

\displaystyle\mathfrak{B}_{2}=

\displaystyle\int_{\Gamma}e^{\prime}(h)\left(-\frac{\partial h}{\partial N}\right)\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{4}\eta_{j}a^{3j}a^{3i}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{4}\eta_{k}a^{3k}\partial_{3}v_{i}\,dS

(7.34)

\displaystyle+\sum_{L=1}^{2}\int_{\Gamma}e^{\prime}(h)\left(-\frac{\partial h}{\partial N}\right)\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{4}\eta_{j}a^{3j}a^{3i}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{4}\eta_{k}a^{Lk}\partial_{L}v_{i}\,dS

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\Gamma}e^{\prime}(h)\left(-\frac{\partial h}{\partial N}\right)\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{4}\eta_{j}a^{3j}a^{3i}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{4}\eta_{k}a^{3k}\partial_{3}v_{i}\,dS-\mathfrak{B}_{3}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle-\mathfrak{B}_{3}+|\partial_{3}ha^{3i}\partial_{3}v_{i}|_{L^{\infty}}\left|\sqrt{e^{\prime}(h)}a^{3i}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{4}\eta_{i}\right|_{0}^{2}.

Combining (
7.32
)-(
7.34
) we get the control of the boundary integral.

\mathfrak{B}\lesssim-\frac{c_{0}}{4}\frac{d}{dt}\left|\sqrt{e^{\prime}(h)}a^{3i}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{4}\eta_{i}\right|_{0}^{2}+\left(\|\partial_{t}h\|_{3}+|\partial_{3}ha^{3i}\partial_{3}v_{i}|_{L^{\infty}}\right)\left|\sqrt{e^{\prime}(h)}a^{3i}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{4}\eta_{i}\right|_{0}^{2}.

(7.35)

It now remains to control
\mathfrak{K}_{0}
and
\mathfrak{K}_{2}
. The reason for us to postpone the proof is that these two terms contain the commutator
\mathfrak{C}(v),\mathfrak{C}(h)
in the Alinhac good unknown. Previously in Section
3.2
we do not have to care too much of it because all the derivatives are spatial in that case. However, the tangential derivative now becomes
\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{4}
and the commutator might contain terms of the form
\partial^{2}\partial_{t}^{3}v
which cannot be controlled.

In
\mathfrak{K}_{0}
, the third term in the commutator
\mathfrak{C}(h)
contributes the following

\mathfrak{K}_{5}:=-\int_{\Omega}e^{\prime}(h)\partial_{t}a^{li}~\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{l}\partial_{t}^{3}h~\mathfrak{V}_{i}\,dy

Note that one can apply Lemma
2.6
to do elliptic estimates on
\left\|\sqrt{e^{\prime}(h)}\partial_{t}^{3}h\right\|_{2}
as in (
7.14
)-(
7.15
) to reduce this term to
\left\|(e^{\prime}(h))^{\frac{3}{2}}\partial_{t}^{5}h\right\|_{0}
which has been controlled by the energy functional of
\partial_{t}^{4}
-differentiated wave equation
7.2
.

\mathfrak{K}_{5}\lesssim\left\|\partial_{t}a\right\|_{L^{\infty}}\left\|(e^{\prime}(h))^{\frac{3}{2}}\partial_{t}^{5}h\right\|_{0}\left\|\sqrt{e^{\prime}(h)}\mathfrak{V}\right\|_{0}

(7.36)

Another term we need to study carefully is
\mathfrak{K}_{2}
, where the commutator
\mathfrak{C}(v)
produce the following terms

\displaystyle-\int_{\Omega}e^{\prime}(h)\mathfrak{H}\partial_{t}a^{li}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{3}\partial_{l}v_{i}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\int_{\Omega}e^{\prime}(h)\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{4}h\partial_{t}a^{mi}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{3}\partial_{m}v_{i}\,dy+\int_{\Omega}e^{\prime}(h)\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{4}\eta_{k}a^{lk}\partial_{l}h\partial_{t}a^{mi}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{3}\partial_{m}v_{i}\,dy

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle\mathfrak{K}_{6}+\mathfrak{K}_{7}.

We should control these two terms under time integral since we have to integrate
\partial_{t}
by parts. For simplicity we only list the most difficult terms. The omitted terms are always of lower order.

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\mathfrak{K}_{6}:=

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}e^{\prime}(h)\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{4}h\partial_{t}a^{mi}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{3}\partial_{m}v_{i}\,dy\,dt

(7.37)

\displaystyle\overset{\overline{\partial}{}}{=}

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}e^{\prime}(h)\overline{\partial}{}^{2}\partial_{t}^{4}h\partial_{t}a^{mi}\partial_{t}^{3}\partial_{m}v_{i}\,dy\,dt+\cdots

\displaystyle\overset{\partial_{t}}{=}

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}e^{\prime}(h)\overline{\partial}{}^{2}\partial_{t}^{3}h\partial_{t}a^{mi}\partial_{t}^{4}\partial_{m}v_{i}\,dy\,dt-\int_{\Omega}e^{\prime}(h)\overline{\partial}{}^{2}\partial_{t}^{3}h\partial_{t}a^{mi}\partial_{t}^{3}\partial_{m}v_{i}\,dy+\cdots

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\left\|\sqrt{e^{\prime}(h)}\overline{\partial}{}^{2}\partial_{t}^{3}h\right\|_{0}\left\|\sqrt{e^{\prime}(h)}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{4}v\right\|_{0}\|\partial_{t}a\|_{L^{\infty}}\,dt+\left\|\sqrt{e^{\prime}(h)}\overline{\partial}{}^{2}\partial_{t}^{3}h\right\|_{0}\left\|\sqrt{e^{\prime}(h)}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{3}v\right\|_{0}\|\partial_{t}a\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}P(\mathfrak{E}(t))\,dt+\delta\left\|(e^{\prime}(h))^{\frac{3}{2}}\partial_{t}^{5}h\right\|_{0}^{2}+\mathcal{P}_{0}+\int_{0}^{T}P(\mathbb{E}(t))\,dt

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\mathfrak{K}_{7}:=

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}e^{\prime}(h)\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{4}\eta_{k}a^{lk}\partial_{l}h\partial_{t}a^{mi}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{3}\partial_{m}v_{i}\,dy\,dt

(7.38)

\displaystyle\overset{\partial_{t}}{=}

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}e^{\prime}(h)\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{5}\eta_{k}a^{lk}\partial_{l}ha^{mi}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{m}\partial_{t}^{2}v_{i}\,dy\,dt+\int_{\Omega}e^{\prime}(h)\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{4}\eta_{k}a^{lk}\partial_{l}ha^{mi}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{m}\partial_{t}^{2}v_{i}\,dy

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}e^{\prime}(h)\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{4}v_{k}a^{lk}\partial_{l}ha^{mi}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{m}\partial_{t}^{2}v_{i}\,dy\,dt+\int_{\Omega}e^{\prime}(h)\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{3}v_{k}a^{lk}\partial_{l}ha^{mi}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{m}\partial_{t}^{2}v_{i}\,dy

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\left\|\sqrt{e^{\prime}(h)}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}\left\|\sqrt{e^{\prime}(h)}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{4}v\right\|_{0}\|a\|_{2}^{2}\|h\|_{3}\|\partial_{t}^{2}v\|_{2}\,dt

\displaystyle+\left\|\sqrt{e^{\prime}(h)}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}\left\|\sqrt{e^{\prime}(h)}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{3}v\right\|_{0}\|a\|_{2}^{2}\|h\|_{3}\|\partial_{t}^{2}v\|_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}P(\mathfrak{E}(t))\,dt+\delta\|\partial_{t}^{2}v\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{1}{4\delta}\left\|\sqrt{e^{\prime}(h)}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}\left\|\sqrt{e^{\prime}(h)}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{3}v\right\|_{0}^{2}\|a\|_{2}^{4}\|h\|_{3}^{2}.

By picking a suitably small
\delta>0
, the
\delta
-term is absorbed by
\mathbb{E}(T)
. The last term can also be absorbed by using
|e^{\prime}(h)|\ll 1
.

Summarizing (
7.24
), (
7.27
)-(
7.31
), (
7.35
)-(
7.38
), we get the estimates for the Alinhac good unknowns
\mathfrak{V}
and
\mathfrak{H}

\left\|\sqrt{e^{\prime}(h)}\mathfrak{V}\right\|_{0}^{2}+\left\|e^{\prime}(h)\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{4}h\right\|_{0}^{2}+\sum_{j=1}^{3}\left\|\sqrt{e^{\prime}(h)}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{4}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\eta\right\|_{0}^{2}+\frac{c_{0}}{4}\left|\sqrt{e^{\prime}(h)}a^{3i}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{4}\eta_{i}\right|_{0}^{2}\lesssim\mathcal{P}_{0}+\int_{0}^{T}P(\mathfrak{E}(t))\,dt.

(7.39)

Finally, by the definition of Alinhac good unknowns (
7.20
), we have

\left\|\sqrt{e^{\prime}(h)}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{4}v\right\|_{0}\lesssim\left\|\sqrt{e^{\prime}(h)}\mathfrak{V}\right\|_{0}+\left\|\sqrt{e^{\prime}(h)}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{3}v\right\|_{0}\|\nabla_{a}v\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim P(\mathbb{E}(T))+\left\|\sqrt{e^{\prime}(h)}\mathfrak{V}\right\|_{0},

and thus we finalize the tangential estimates as

\displaystyle\left\|\sqrt{e^{\prime}(h)}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{4}v\right\|_{0}^{2}+\left\|e^{\prime}(h)\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{4}h\right\|_{0}^{2}+\sum_{j=1}^{3}\left\|\sqrt{e^{\prime}(h)}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{4}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\eta\right\|_{0}^{2}+\frac{c_{0}}{4}\left|\sqrt{e^{\prime}(h)}a^{3i}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{4}\eta_{i}\right|_{0}^{2}

(7.40)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{0}+\mathfrak{E}(0)+\int_{0}^{T}\mathfrak{E}(t)^{2}\,dt.

7.4
A posteriori enhanced regularity of full time derivatives

Combining Lemma
2.5
and divergence estimate (
7.16
), curl estimate (
7.19
) and tangential estimates (
7.40
), we are able to get the enhanced regularity of full time derivatives.

\displaystyle\left\|\sqrt{e^{\prime}(h)}\partial_{t}^{4}v\right\|_{1}^{2}+\left\|e^{\prime}(h)\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{4}h\right\|_{0}^{2}+\sum_{j=1}^{3}\left\|\sqrt{e^{\prime}(h)}\partial_{t}^{4}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\eta\right\|_{1}^{2}+\frac{c_{0}}{4}\left|\sqrt{e^{\prime}(h)}a^{3i}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{4}\eta_{i}\right|_{0}^{2}

(7.41)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\left\|(e^{\prime}(h))^{\frac{3}{2}}\partial_{t}^{5}h\right\|_{0}^{2}+\left\|e^{\prime}(h)\partial_{t}^{4}h\right\|_{1}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{0}+\mathfrak{E}(0)+\int_{0}^{T}P(\mathfrak{E}(t))\,dt,

and thus by Gronwall’s inequality, there exists some
0<T_{1}<T
such that

\sup_{0\leq t\leq T_{1}}\mathfrak{E}(t)\leq\mathcal{P}_{0}\mathbb{E}(0).

(7.42)

Such estimate recovers the energy bound in Lindblad-Luo
[
60
]
and Luo
[
62
]
. This demonstrates that our proof is completely applicable to the study of compressible Euler equations. To finalize to proof of Theorem
1.3
, it remains to construct the initial data satisfying the compatibility conditions (
1.10
) up to 5-th order such that
\mathfrak{E}(0)\leq\mathcal{P}_{0}
. This will be proved in Section
8
.

8
Construction of initial data satisfying the compatibility conditions

The last section of this manuscript presents the construction of initial data satisfying the compatibility conditions (
1.10
) up to 5-th order. The compatibility conditions come from the boundary conditions and higher order wave equations. We start the initial data
(\textbf{v}_{0},\mathbf{G}^{0},Q_{0})
of free-boundary incompressible elastodynamic equations:
\textbf{v}_{0}
is a divergence-free vector field and
\mathbf{G}^{0}
is a divergence-free matrix in the sense of
\partial_{k}\mathbf{G}^{0}_{kj}=0
, then
Q_{0}
is defined by the elliptic system
-\Delta Q_{0}=(\partial_{i}\textbf{v}_{0}^{k})(\partial_{k}\textbf{v}_{0}^{i})+\sum\limits_{j=1}^{3}(\partial_{i}\mathbf{G}_{kj}^{0})(\partial_{k}\mathbf{G}_{ij}^{0})
subject to
Q_{0}|_{\Gamma}=0
and also the Rayleigh-Taylor sign condition
-\frac{\partial Q_{0}}{\partial n}|_{\Gamma}\geq c_{0}>0
. We are going to construct a sequence of compressible data
(v_{0}^{\varepsilon},\mathbf{F}^{0}_{\varepsilon},\mathfrak{h}_{0}^{\varepsilon})
satisfying the compatibility conditions (
1.10
) up to 5-th order and strongly converging to
(\textbf{v}_{0},\mathbf{G}^{0},Q_{0})
as the sound speed
\varepsilon\to+\infty
. For simplicity
5
5

5

The proof in general case can be similarly proceeded. See Luo
[
62
]
.
we suppose that
e(\mathfrak{h})=\varepsilon^{-1}h
in order to omit the smaller and lower order terms in the wave equation of
h
. Then the compressible elastodynamic equations at time 0 (
\eta=
Id) becomes

\begin{cases}\partial_{t}v=-\partial h+\sum\limits_{j=1}^{3}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)^{2}\eta~~~&\text{in }\Omega,\\
\text{div }v=-\varepsilon^{-1}\partial_{t}h~~~&\text{in }\Omega,\\
\partial_{t}F_{j}=(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)v~~~&\text{in }\Omega,\\
\text{div }F_{j}:=\partial_{k}F_{kj}=-\varepsilon^{-1}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)h~~~&\text{in }\Omega,\\
\partial_{t}|_{\Gamma}\in\mathcal{T}([0,T]\times\Gamma)~~~&\text{on }\Gamma,\\
h=0~~~&\text{on }\Gamma,\\
\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot N=0~~~&\text{on }\Gamma,\\
-\frac{\partial\mathfrak{h}_{0}}{\partial N}\geq c_{0}>0~~~&\text{on }\Gamma.\end{cases}

(8.1)

8.1
Compatibility conditions and constraints on the initial data

From (
8.1
) we find that the initial data
(v_{0},\mathfrak{h}_{0})
should satisfy

\displaystyle\mathfrak{h}_{0}|_{\Gamma}=0,~~\text{div }v_{0}|_{\Gamma}=0,~~\text{div }\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}|_{\Gamma}=0

(8.2)

since
\partial_{t}
and
(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)
are tangential derivatives on the boundary
\Gamma
.

We now define
h_{(k)}:=\partial_{t}^{k}h|_{t=0},~v_{(k)}:=\partial_{t}^{k}v|_{t=0}
and
F_{(k)}:=\partial_{t}^{k}F|_{t=0}
. Recall the wave equation of
h
now becomes the following equation at
t=0

\varepsilon^{-1}\partial_{t}^{2}h-\Delta h_{0}=(\partial_{i}v_{0}^{k})(\partial_{k}v_{0}^{i})-\sum_{j=1}^{3}(\partial_{i}\mathbf{F}^{0}_{kj})(\partial_{k}\mathbf{F}^{0}_{ij})+\varepsilon^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{3}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)^{2}h_{0},

(8.3)

and thus

-\Delta\mathfrak{h}_{0}=-\varepsilon^{-1}h_{(2)}+\varepsilon^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{3}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)^{2}\mathfrak{h}_{0}+\underbrace{(\partial_{i}v_{0}^{k})(\partial_{k}v_{0}^{i})-\sum_{j=1}^{3}(\partial_{i}\mathbf{F}^{0}_{kj})(\partial_{k}\mathbf{F}^{0}_{ij})}_{\mathfrak{N}_{0}},

(8.4)

So we also have

\displaystyle\Delta\mathfrak{h}_{0}+\mathfrak{N}_{0}=0~~\text{ on }\Gamma.

(8.5)

Time differentiating the wave equation yields that

-\Delta h_{(1)}=-\varepsilon^{-1}h_{(3)}+\varepsilon^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{3}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)^{2}h_{(1)}+\mathfrak{N}_{1},

(8.6)

and thus we must have

\displaystyle\Delta h_{(1)}+\mathfrak{N}_{1}=0~~\text{ on }\Gamma.

(8.7)

Here
\mathfrak{N}_{1}
denotes the nonlinear quantities of
\partial_{t}\mathfrak{N}_{0}|_{t=0}
.

Repeat this step, we get
\partial_{t}^{k}
-differentiated wave equation at time
t=0

-\Delta h_{(k)}=-\varepsilon^{-1}h_{(k+2)}+\varepsilon^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{3}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)^{2}h_{(k)}+\mathfrak{N}_{k},

(8.8)

and thus we must have

\displaystyle\Delta h_{(k)}+\mathfrak{N}_{k}=0~~\text{ on }\Gamma.

(8.9)

Here
\mathfrak{N}_{k}
denotes the nonlinear quantities of
\partial_{t}\mathfrak{N}_{k-1}|_{t=0}
.

8.2
Construction of initial data

Now we construct the compressible data based on the incompressible data. Let
(\textbf{v}_{0},\mathbf{G}^{0},Q_{0})
be the incompressible data and then we define

\displaystyle v_{0}=

\displaystyle\textbf{v}_{0}+\partial\phi,

(8.10)

\displaystyle\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}=

\displaystyle\mathbf{G}^{0}_{j}+\partial\varphi_{j}.

(8.11)

Therefore the continuity equation, the divergence constraints and the boundary condition of
\mathbf{F}
yield the following equations

\displaystyle-\Delta\phi=\varepsilon^{-1}h_{(1)},

\displaystyle~~~\frac{\partial\phi}{\partial N}|_{\Gamma}=0

(8.12)

\displaystyle-\Delta\varphi_{j}=\varepsilon^{-1}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\mathfrak{h}_{0}=\varepsilon^{-1}\partial_{k}\varphi_{j}\partial_{k}\mathfrak{h}_{0},

\displaystyle~~~\frac{\partial\varphi_{j}}{\partial N}|_{\Gamma}=0.

(8.13)

Then (
8.4
), (
8.6
), (
8.8
) require that for
k=0,1,\cdots,m

\begin{cases}-\Delta h_{(k)}=-\varepsilon^{-1}h_{(k+2)}+\varepsilon^{-1}\sum\limits_{j=1}^{3}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)^{2}h_{(k)}+\mathfrak{N}_{k}~~~&\text{ in }\Omega,\\
h_{(k)}=0~~~&\text{ on }\Gamma.\end{cases}

(8.14)

Here
\mathfrak{N}_{k}
is a function of
v_{0},\mathbf{F}^{0},\mathfrak{h}_{0},h_{(1)},\cdots,h_{(k-1)}
and their spatial derivatives. If we set
h_{(m+1)}=h_{(m+2)}=0
. Then (
8.10
)-(
8.14
) gives a system of
(v_{0},\mathbf{F}^{0},\mathfrak{h}_{0},h_{(1)},\cdots,h_{(N)})
such that the compressible data
(v_{0},\mathbf{F}^{0},\mathfrak{h}_{0})
strongly converges to the incompressible data as
\varepsilon\to+\infty
and for each
\varepsilon>0
the compressible data satisfies the compatibility conditions up to order
m
. Then one can invoke
\partial_{t}v=-\partial h+\sum\limits_{j=1}^{3}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}
and
\partial_{t}\mathbf{F}_{j}=(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)v
to recover
v_{(k)}
and
\mathbf{F}_{(k)}
in terms of
(v_{0},\mathbf{F}^{0},\mathfrak{h}_{0},h_{(1)},\cdots,h_{(N)})
. Thus, it remains to show that the system (
8.10
)-(
8.14
) has a solution if
\varepsilon
is suitably large with uniform-in-
\varepsilon
energy bound.

8.3
Existence of initial data satisfying the compatibility conditions

In the previous proof of local well-posedness, incompressible limit and enhaced regularity, we need to find the compressible data satisfying the compatibility conditions up to 5-th order. According to the analysis above, we need to solve the following elliptic system

\begin{cases}v_{0}=\textbf{v}_{0}+\partial\phi~~~&\text{ in }\Omega,\\
\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}=\mathbf{G}^{0}_{j}+\partial\varphi_{j}~~~&\text{ in }\Omega,\\
-\Delta\phi=\varepsilon^{-1}h_{(1)}~~~&\text{ in }\Omega,\\
-\Delta\varphi_{j}=\varepsilon^{-1}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)\mathfrak{h}_{0}~~~&\text{ in }\Omega,\\
-\Delta h_{(k)}=-\varepsilon^{-1}h_{(k+2)}+\varepsilon^{-1}\sum\limits_{j=1}^{3}(\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}\cdot\partial)^{2}h_{(k)}+\mathfrak{N}_{k}~~~&\text{ in }\Omega,~0\leq k\leq 3\\
h_{(k)}=0,\frac{\partial\phi}{\partial N}=\frac{\partial\varphi_{j}}{\partial N}=0~~~&\text{ on }\Gamma,\\
h_{(4)}=h_{(5)}=0~~~&\text{ in }\Omega.\\
\end{cases}

(8.15)

Here the quantity
\mathfrak{N}_{k}
has the following expression

\mathfrak{N}_{k}=\sum C^{\alpha_{1}\cdots\alpha_{m}\beta_{1}\cdots\beta_{n}\gamma_{1}\cdots\gamma_{p}}_{\lambda_{1}\cdots\lambda_{p},k}(\partial^{\alpha_{1}}v_{0})\cdots(\partial^{\alpha_{m}}v_{0})(\partial^{\beta_{1}}\mathbf{F}^{0})\cdots(\partial^{\beta_{n}}\mathbf{F}^{0})(\partial^{\gamma_{1}}h_{(\lambda_{1})})\cdots(\partial^{\gamma_{p}}h_{(\lambda_{p})}),

(8.16)

where

\displaystyle\alpha_{1}+\cdots+\alpha_{m}+\beta_{1}+\cdots+\beta_{n}+(\gamma_{1}+\lambda_{1})+\cdots+(\gamma_{p}+\lambda_{p})=k+2,

\displaystyle 0\leq\alpha_{i},\beta_{i},(\gamma_{i}+\lambda_{i})\leq k+1,0\leq\lambda_{i}\leq k-1.

We compute
\mathfrak{N}_{0},\mathfrak{N}_{1}
for example to illustrate how we get such a formula. When
k=0
, (
8.4
) shows that

\mathfrak{N}_{0}=(\partial v_{0})(\partial v_{0})-(\partial\mathbf{F}^{0})(\partial\mathbf{F}^{0}).

When
k=1
, we have
\mathfrak{N}_{1}=(\partial v_{(1)})(\partial v_{0})-(\partial\mathbf{F}_{(1)})(\partial\mathbf{F}^{0})
. Invoking (
8.1
) we get
v_{(1)}=-\partial\mathfrak{h}_{0}+(\mathbf{F}^{0}\cdot\partial)\mathbf{F}^{0}
and
\mathbf{F}_{(1)}=(\mathbf{F}^{0}\cdot\partial)v_{0}
, and thus

\mathfrak{N}_{1}=-(\partial^{2}\mathfrak{h}_{0})(\partial v_{0})+\left(\mathbf{F}^{0}\cdot(\partial^{2}\mathbf{F}^{0})+(\partial\mathbf{F}^{0})(\partial\mathbf{F}^{0})\right)(\partial v_{0})-\mathbf{F}^{0}\cdot(\partial^{2}v_{0})(\partial\mathbf{F}^{0}).

The expression of
\mathfrak{N}_{k}
can also be similarly computed and we omit the details.

8.3.1
A priori estimates

We first prove the uniform-in-
\varepsilon
a priori estimate for the elliptic system (
8.15
). This is necessary for us to do iteration to construct the solution to (
8.15
).

By standard elliptic estimate one has

\|\partial\phi\|_{5}\approx\|\Delta\phi\|_{4}\lesssim\varepsilon^{-1}\|h_{(1)}\|_{4},

(8.17)

and thus

\|v_{0}\|_{5}\lesssim\|\textbf{v}_{0}\|_{5}+\varepsilon^{-1}\|h_{(1)}\|_{4}.

(8.18)

Similarly one has

\|\partial\varphi_{j}\|_{5}\approx\|\Delta\varphi_{j}\|_{4}\lesssim\varepsilon^{-1}\|\mathbf{F}^{0}\|_{4}\|\mathfrak{h}_{0}\|_{5},

(8.19)

and

\|\mathbf{F}^{0}\|_{5}\lesssim\|\mathbf{G}^{0}\|_{5}+\varepsilon^{-1}\|\mathbf{F}^{0}\|_{4}\|\mathfrak{h}_{0}\|_{5}.

(8.20)

Then we estimate
\mathfrak{h}_{0}
by

\|\mathfrak{h}_{0}\|_{5}\lesssim\varepsilon^{-1}\left(\|h_{(2)}\|_{3}+\|\mathfrak{h}_{0}\|_{5}\|\mathbf{F}^{0}\|_{3}^{2}\right)+\|v_{0}\|_{4}^{2}+\|\mathbf{F}^{0}\|_{4}^{2}.

(8.21)

This together with (
8.18
) and (
8.20
) gives

\|\mathfrak{h}_{0}\|_{5}\lesssim\|\textbf{v}_{0}\|_{4}^{2}+\|\mathbf{G}^{0}\|_{4}^{2}+\varepsilon^{-1}P\left(\|h_{(1)}\|_{3},\|h_{(2)}\|_{3},\|\mathfrak{h}_{0}\|_{5},\|\mathbf{F}^{0}\|_{4}\right).

(8.22)

Invoking the first and third equations in (
8.1
) we get

\displaystyle\|v_{(1)}\|_{4}\leq

\displaystyle\|\mathfrak{h}_{0}\|_{5}+\|(\mathbf{F}^{0}\cdot\partial)\mathbf{F}^{0}\|_{4}\lesssim P\left(\|\mathfrak{h}_{0}\|_{5},\|\mathbf{F}^{0}\|_{5}\right)

(8.23)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle P(\|\textbf{w}_{0}\|_{5},\|\mathbf{G}^{0}\|_{5})+\varepsilon^{-1}P\left(\|\mathfrak{h}_{0}\|_{5},\|h_{(1)}\|_{4},\|\mathbf{F}^{0}\|_{4}\right),

and

\displaystyle\|F_{(1)}\|_{4}\leq

\displaystyle\|(\mathbf{F}^{0}\cdot\partial)v_{0}\|_{4}\lesssim\|\mathbf{F}^{0}\|_{4}\|v_{0}\|_{5}

(8.24)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle P(\|\textbf{w}_{0}\|_{5},\|\mathbf{G}^{0}\|_{4})+\varepsilon^{-1}P\left(\|\mathfrak{h}_{0}\|_{4},\|h_{(1)}\|_{4},\|\mathbf{F}^{0}\|_{3}\right).

Next we analyze
h_{(1)}
. By elliptic estimates and previous estimates, we have

\displaystyle\|h_{(1)}\|_{4}\lesssim

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{-1}\left(\|h_{(3)}\|_{2}+\|\mathbf{F}^{0}\|_{3}\|\mathbf{F}^{0}\|_{2}\|h_{(1)}\|_{4}\right)+\|v_{0}\|_{3}\|v_{(1)}\|_{3}+\|\mathbf{F}^{0}\|_{3}\|F_{(1)}\|_{3}

(8.25)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{-1}P\left(\|h_{(3)}\|_{2},\|h_{(2)}\|_{3},\|h_{(1)}\|_{4},\|\mathfrak{h}_{0}\|_{5},\|\mathbf{F}^{0}\|_{4}\right)+P(\|\textbf{w}_{0}\|_{5},\|\mathbf{G}^{0}\|_{4}).

Again invoking the
\partial_{t}
-differentiated first and third equations in (
8.1
) we get

\displaystyle\|v_{(2)}\|_{3}\leq

\displaystyle\|h_{(1)}\|_{4}+\|(\mathbf{F}^{0}\cdot\partial)F_{(1)}\|_{3}\lesssim\|h_{(1)}\|_{4}+\|\mathbf{F}^{0}\|_{3}\|F_{1}\|_{4}

(8.26)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{-1}P\left(\|h_{(3)}\|_{2},\|h_{(2)}\|_{3},\|\mathfrak{h}_{0}\|_{5},\|\mathbf{F}^{0}\|_{4}\right)+P(\|\textbf{w}_{0}\|_{5},\|\mathbf{G}^{0}\|_{4}),

and

\displaystyle\|F_{(2)}\|_{3}\lesssim\varepsilon^{-1}P\left(\|\mathfrak{h}_{0}\|_{5},\|\mathbf{F}^{0}\|_{3}\right)+P(\|\textbf{w}_{0}\|_{5},\|\mathbf{G}^{0}\|_{4}).

(8.27)

Once again we use elliptic estimates and (
8.23
)-(
8.27
) and
h_{(4)}=0
to get

\displaystyle\|h_{(2)}\|_{3}\lesssim

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{-1}P\left(\|h_{(3)}\|_{2},\|h_{(2)}\|_{3},\|h_{(1)}\|_{4},\|\mathfrak{h}_{0}\|_{5},\|\mathbf{F}^{0}\|_{4}\right)+P(\|\textbf{w}_{0}\|_{5},\|\mathbf{G}^{0}\|_{4}).

(8.28)

Again invoking the
\partial_{t}^{2}
-differentiated first and third equations in (
8.1
) we get

\displaystyle\|v_{(3)}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{-1}P\left(\|h_{(3)}\|_{2},\|h_{(2)}\|_{3},\|h_{(1)}\|_{4},\|\mathfrak{h}_{0}\|_{5},\|\mathbf{F}^{0}\|_{4}\right)+P(\|\textbf{w}_{0}\|_{5},\|\mathbf{G}^{0}\|_{4}),

(8.29)

and

\displaystyle\|F_{(3)}\|_{2}\leq\|(\mathbf{F}^{0}\cdot\partial)v_{(2)}\|_{2}\lesssim\varepsilon^{-1}P\left(\|h_{(3)}\|_{2},\|h_{(2)}\|_{3},\|h_{(1)}\|_{4},\|\mathfrak{h}_{0}\|_{5},\|\mathbf{F}^{0}\|_{4}\right)+P(\|\textbf{w}_{0}\|_{5},\|\mathbf{G}^{0}\|_{4}).

(8.30)

Next we use elliptic estimates and (
8.28
)-(
8.30
) and
h_{(5)}=0
to get

\displaystyle\|h_{(3)}\|_{2}\lesssim

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{-1}P\left(\|h_{(3)}\|_{2},\|h_{(2)}\|_{3},\|h_{(1)}\|_{4},\|\mathfrak{h}_{0}\|_{5},\|\mathbf{F}^{0}\|_{4}\right)+P(\|\textbf{w}_{0}\|_{5},\|\mathbf{G}^{0}\|_{4}).

(8.31)

Again invoking the
\partial_{t}^{3}
-differentiated first and third equations in (
8.1
) we get

\displaystyle\|v_{(4)}\|_{1}

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{-1}P\left(\|h_{(3)}\|_{2},\|h_{(2)}\|_{3},\|h_{(1)}\|_{4},\|\mathfrak{h}_{0}\|_{5},\|\mathbf{F}^{0}\|_{4}\right)+P(\|\textbf{w}_{0}\|_{5},\|\mathbf{G}^{0}\|_{4}),

(8.32)

and

\displaystyle\|F_{(4)}\|_{1}\lesssim\varepsilon^{-1}P\left(\|h_{(3)}\|_{2},\|h_{(2)}\|_{3},\|h_{(1)}\|_{4},\|\mathfrak{h}_{0}\|_{5},\|\mathbf{F}^{0}\|_{4}\right)+P(\|\textbf{w}_{0}\|_{5},\|\mathbf{G}^{0}\|_{4}).

(8.33)

Define the energy

\mathfrak{E}_{I}:=\sum_{k=0}^{4}\|v_{(k)}\|_{5-k}^{2}+\|F_{(k)}\|_{5-k}^{2}+\|h_{(k)}\|_{5-k}^{2}.

(8.34)

Then our computation above shows that

\mathfrak{E}_{I}\lesssim\varepsilon^{-1}P(\mathfrak{E}_{I})+P(\|\textbf{w}_{0}\|_{3},\|\mathbf{G}^{0}\|_{4}),

and thus by choosing
\varepsilon>0
suitably large we have proved that

\mathfrak{E}_{I}\leq P(\|\textbf{w}_{0}\|_{5},\|\mathbf{G}^{0}\|_{4}),

(8.35)

which is the uniform-in-
\epsilon
estimates for the elliptic system (
8.15
).

8.3.2
Existence by iteration scheme

Having the a priori estimates in hand, it remains to proceed the standard iteration scheme. We start from the solution
(\mathfrak{h}_{0}^{(0)},h_{(1)}^{(0)},\cdots,h_{(4)}^{(0)})
which solves

-\Delta h_{(k)}^{(0)}=\mathfrak{N}_{k}(\partial^{\alpha}\textbf{v}_{0},\partial^{\beta}\mathbf{F}^{0},\partial^{\gamma_{0}}\mathfrak{h}_{0}^{(0)},\cdots,\partial^{\gamma_{k-1}}h_{(k-1)}^{(0)}),~~0\leq k\leq 3.

(8.36)

Then we inductively define
(\mathfrak{h}_{0}^{(m)},h_{(1)}^{(m)},\cdots,h_{(3)}^{(m)})
by

\begin{cases}v_{0}^{(n)}=\textbf{v}_{0}+\partial\phi^{(n)}~~~&\text{ in }\Omega,\\
{\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}}^{(n)}=\mathbf{G}^{0}_{j}+\partial\varphi_{j}^{(n)}~~~&\text{ in }\Omega,\\
-\Delta\phi^{(n)}=\varepsilon^{-1}h_{(1)}^{(n)}~~~&\text{ in }\Omega,\\
-\Delta\varphi_{j}^{(n)}=\varepsilon^{-1}({\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}}^{(n)}\cdot\partial)\mathfrak{h}_{0}^{(n)}~~~&\text{ in }\Omega,\\
-\Delta h_{(k)}^{(n)}=-\varepsilon^{-1}h_{(k+2)}^{(n)}+\varepsilon^{-1}\sum\limits_{j=1}^{3}({\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}}^{(n)}\cdot\partial)^{2}h_{(k)}^{(n)}+\mathfrak{N}_{k}^{(n)}~~~&\text{ in }\Omega,~0\leq k\leq 3\\
h_{(k)}^{(n)}=0,\frac{\partial\phi^{(n)}}{\partial N}=\frac{\partial\varphi_{j}^{(n)}}{\partial N}=0~~~&\text{ on }\Gamma,\\
h_{(4)}^{(n)}=h_{(5)}^{(n)}=0~~~&\text{ in }\Omega,\end{cases}

(8.37)

where
\mathfrak{N}_{k}^{(n)}
satisfies the form (
8.16
).

Define the difference

\displaystyle[\phi]^{(n)}:=

\displaystyle\phi^{(n)}-\phi^{(n-1)},~~[\varphi_{j}]^{(n)}:=\varphi_{j}^{(n)}-\varphi_{j}^{(n-1)},

\displaystyle[v]_{(k)}^{(n)}:=

\displaystyle v_{(k)}^{(n)}-v_{(k)}^{(n-1)},[\mathbf{F}]_{(k)}^{(n)}:=\mathbf{F}_{(k)}^{(n)}-\mathbf{F}_{(k)}^{(n-1)},[h]_{(k)}^{(n)}:=h_{(k)}^{(n)}-h_{(k)}^{(n-1)},

\displaystyle[\mathfrak{N}]_{k}^{(n)}:=

\displaystyle\mathfrak{N}_{k}^{(n)}-\mathfrak{N}_{k}^{(n-1)},

\displaystyle[\mathfrak{E}_{I}]^{(n)}:=

\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{4}\|v_{(k)}^{(n)}\|_{5-k}^{2}+\|\mathbf{F}_{(k)}^{(n)}\|_{5-k}^{2}+\|h_{(k)}^{(n)}\|_{5-k}^{2}.

Then we have

\begin{cases}[v]_{0}^{(n)}=\partial[\phi]^{(n)}~~~&\text{ in }\Omega,\\
{[\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}]}^{(n)}=\partial[\varphi_{j}]^{(n)}~~~&\text{ in }\Omega,\\
-\Delta[\phi]^{(n)}=\varepsilon^{-1}[h]_{(1)}^{(n)}~~~&\text{ in }\Omega,\\
-\Delta[\varphi_{j}]^{(n)}=\varepsilon^{-1}({[\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}]}^{(n)}\cdot\partial)\mathfrak{h}_{0}^{(n)}+\varepsilon^{-1}({\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}}^{(n-1)}\cdot\partial)[\mathfrak{h}]_{0}^{(n)}~~~&\text{ in }\Omega,\\
-\Delta[h]_{(k)}^{(n)}=-\varepsilon^{-1}[h]_{(k+2)}^{(n)}+\varepsilon^{-1}\sum\limits_{j=1}^{3}\bigg(({[\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}]}^{(n)}\cdot\partial)({\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}}^{(n)}\cdot\partial)h_{(k)}^{(n)}~~~\\
~~~~~~~~+({\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}}^{(n-1)}\cdot\partial)({[\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}]}^{(n)}\cdot\partial)h_{(k)}^{(n)}+({\mathbf{F}^{0}_{j}}^{(n-1)}\cdot\partial)^{2}[h]_{(k)}^{(n)}\bigg)+[\mathfrak{N}]_{k}^{(n)}~~~&\text{ in }\Omega,~0\leq k\leq 3\\
[h]_{(k)}^{(n)}=0,\frac{\partial\phi^{(n)}}{\partial N}=\frac{\partial\varphi_{j}^{(n)}}{\partial N}=0~~~&\text{ on }\Gamma,\\
[h]_{(4)}^{(n)}=[h]_{(5)}^{(n)}=0~~~&\text{ in }\Omega,\end{cases}

(8.38)

Following the same method as in the a priori estimates, we are able to prove that

[\mathfrak{E}_{I}]^{(n)}\lesssim\varepsilon^{-1}P(\|\textbf{v}_{0}\|_{5},\|\mathbf{G}^{0}\|_{5})[\mathfrak{E}_{I}]^{(n-1)},

and inductively we can get

[\mathfrak{E}_{I}]^{(n)}\lesssim\varepsilon^{-1}P(\|\textbf{v}_{0}\|_{5},\|\mathbf{G}^{0}\|_{5})^{(n)}[\mathfrak{E}_{I}]^{(0)}.

By choosing a suitably large
\varepsilon>0
such that
\varepsilon^{-1}P(\|\textbf{v}_{0}\|_{5},\|\mathbf{G}^{0}\|_{5})<0.99
, we finally prove that

[\mathfrak{E}_{I}]^{(n)}+\cdots+[\mathfrak{E}_{I}]^{(n+m)}\to 0

as
m,n\to+\infty
. The iteration scheme is finished and thus the existence of the solution to the elliptic system (
8.15
), i.e., Theorem
1.4
is proven.
